a pointwise divisor bound for the cofactor $q\leq\ell^B$ of the top prime
power, the range of $\lcm$'s and the range $v\leq1+B$ of exponents.
Everything else is $B$-free: Theorems~\ref{thm:kcomp} and~\ref{thm:wseq}
are arithmetic-free and have no arity bound, the base enters only
through its $R$-term, and every class is decided at its top prime.  The
number of prime factors of a modulus enters nowhere: the level gives
$d$-locality with $d=\lfloor\lambda/s\rfloor$ block primes per term of
$\nu$, the first moments are sums over \emph{all} classes with top prime in
a range, and the second moment is a sum over all cofactors
\cite[Remark~5.3]{KA2}.  So it suffices to bound these three sums over
all moduli, and to find a base that avoids the small classes of every
type.

\subsection{The four class types and the square base}
\begin{definition}[forced-class families {\cite[Def.~2.0]{KA2}}]\label{def:types}
A \emph{class} is a residue class $b\pmod G$.  The four \emph{types} are:
\begin{itemize}
\item $\cR(M)$-classes $-4D\pmod M$, $M\equiv3\pmod4$, $D\mid A_M^2$,
  $A_M=(M+1)/4$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:RM});
\item $(a,D)$-classes $-(4D+a)\pmod{4a\,g(D)}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:aD});
\item Case-A classes $-m^{-1}\pmod{4rh}$, where $d=rh^2$ with $r$
  squarefree, so that $g(d)=rh$, and $m\mid4d+1$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:caseA});
\item \emph{selector classes} $0\pmod p$, $p$ prime.
\end{itemize}
The first three types are forced; selector classes are not.  They encode
rough-number restrictions: removing $0\bmod p$ for all $p\leq y$ turns
the avoider set $\A$ into $\A\cap\{(n,P_y)=1\}$, the selector of
Definition~\ref{def:slice} and of \cite{TQ}.  A \emph{family} $\cG$ is
any finite set of classes of these types, mixed arbitrarily, with no
condition on the moduli: no $B$, no dominant prime, any number and
multiplicity of prime factors at any scale.  Its \emph{avoider set}
$\A(\cG)$ is the set of \textbf{all} integers in none of its classes.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[no squares; {\cite[Lemmas~2.1, 2.2]{KA2}}; \lab{proved}]\label{lem:nosquare}
The following hold.
\begin{enumerate}
\item No residue of a class of $\cR(M)$ is a square modulo~$M$.
\item No $(a,D)$-class contains a perfect square $x^2\geq1$.  Hence
  no residue of an $(a,D)$-class is a square modulo its modulus.
\item No residue of a Case-A class $-m^{-1}\pmod{4rh}$ is a square modulo
  $4rh$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
(1) If $-4D\equiv x^2\pmod M$, then $(x,M)=1$ since $(D,M)=1$, and
$(\frac{-4D}M)=(\frac{x^2}M)=1$, against Lemma~\ref{lem:mordell}.
(2) If $n=x^2\geq1$ lies in the class, the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:aD}
puts $n$ in a class of $\cR(M)$ with $M=(n+4D)/a$; by (1) $n$ is not a
square modulo $M$, a contradiction.  If a residue $c$ of the class were
a square modulo the modulus, $c\equiv x^2$ with $1\leq x\leq 4ag(D)$,
then the perfect square $x^2$ would lie in the class.
(3) Here $(m,2rh)=1$ since $m\mid4rh^2+1$.  Suppose
$-m^{-1}\equiv y^2\pmod{4rh}$.  Then $y$ is odd, so $y^2\equiv1\pmod8$ and
$m\equiv-1\pmod4$; in particular $(\frac{-1}m)=-1$, and if $r$ is even then
$8\mid4rh$, $m\equiv-1\pmod8$ and $(\frac2m)=1$.  For each odd prime
$p\mid r$, $y^2m\equiv-1\pmod p$ gives $(\frac{-m}p)=1$; so the Jacobi symbol
$(\frac{-m}{r_o})=1$, where $r_o$ is the odd part of $r$.  Modulo $m$,
$4rh^2\equiv-1$ gives $(\frac{-r}m)=1$.  On the other hand
$(\frac{-r}m)=-(\frac{r_o}m)$, and reciprocity with $(m-1)/2$ odd gives
$(\frac{r_o}m)=(\frac m{r_o})(-1)^{(r_o-1)/2}
=(\frac{-1}{r_o})(\frac{-m}{r_o})(-1)^{(r_o-1)/2}=1$.  So
$(\frac{-r}m)=-1$, a contradiction ($r_o=1$ included).
\end{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:nosquare}(2)--(3) proves an observation that \cite{TW}
had made only numerically; \cite[\S7]{KA2} re-checks it by brute force
\lab{evidence}.  It is consistent with Schinzel's theorem, as quoted
in~\cite{ElsholtzTao}, that no polynomial identity covers a class
$b\bmod a$ with $b$ a quadratic residue; we have not used or checked
that theorem.

\begin{lemma}[square base; {\cite[Lemma~2.3]{KA2}}; \lab{proved}]\label{lem:squarebase}
Fix $W\geq3$, let $P_W$ be the product of the primes $\leq W$, and let
$Q_0$ be the least common multiple of $8P_W$ and
of the $W$-smooth parts of all moduli of $\cG$, so that $Q_0$ is
$W$-smooth.  Put
\[
 R_W^\square=\{c\bmod Q_0:\ c\bmod p^e\text{ is a unit square for every }p^e\,\|\,Q_0\}.
\]
\begin{enumerate}
\item Every $c\in R_W^\square$ avoids every class $b\pmod G$ with $G$
  $W$-smooth that contains no unit square modulo $G$; in particular
  every $W$-smooth class of $\cG$, of all four types.
\item $\log(Q_0/|R_W^\square|)=3\log2+\sum_{3\leq p\leq W}\log\frac{2p}{p-1}\leq2W$.
\item Under the uniform law on $R_W^\square$, residues at distinct primes
  are independent, and $\Prob(c\equiv a\pmod{p^e})\leq\gamma(p)/p^e$ for
  $p^e\mid Q_0$, with $\gamma(p)=2p/(p-1)$ for odd $p$ and $\gamma(2)=8$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
(1) Every $p^e\,\|\,G$ divides $Q_0$, so $c\equiv b\pmod G$ would make $b$ a
unit square modulo $G$ by CRT.  For the first three types this
contradicts Lemma~\ref{lem:nosquare}, which excludes even non-unit
squares; a selector class $0\pmod p$ contains no unit.  (2) and (3): by
CRT $R_W^\square$ is a product; the unit squares are a fraction
$(p-1)/(2p)$ of the residues modulo $p^e$ for odd $p$, and $1/8$ modulo
$2^e$, $e\geq3$.
\end{proof}
This replaces the quadratic-residue base $R_W$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:QR},
which rests on Lemma~\ref{lem:mordell} and so serves $\cR(M)$ only.  The
inflation weights become $\Gamma(m)=\prod_{p\mid m}\gamma'(p)$ with
$\gamma'=\gamma$ for $p\leq W$ and $\gamma'(\ell)=(1-\ell^{-1/2})^{-1}$ for
$\ell>W$, and $Q'(n\equiv b\pmod m)\leq\Gamma(m)/m$ for all $m,b$, as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:kmoments}(1).

\subsection{Moments without \texorpdfstring{$B$}{B}}
For a class $C=(b\bmod G)$ write $P(C)=P(G)$, and put
\[
 \mathfrak M(y)=\sum_{C\in\mathfrak U,\ W<P(C)\leq y}\frac{\Gamma(G)}G,
\]
where $\mathfrak U$ is the universe of all classes of the four types (each
counted once per parametrisation).  As in Lemma~\ref{lem:kmoments}(2),
$\E_{Q'}\sum_{\ell\in V}p_\ell\leq\mathfrak M(\max V)$ for every block $V$ and
every family $\cG\subseteq\mathfrak U$.

The bounds in this subsection are from \cite{KA3}; its hostile review
\cite{KA3rev} found every claim sound after five minor repairs, which are
incorporated here.  They replace the first-moment bound of the previous
version of this note \cite[Lemmas~3.1--3.6, Cor.~3.7]{KA2}, which lost a
factor $(\log\log y)^3$ (Remark~\ref{rem:rankinloss}).  Write
$\Gamma=1*h$ with $h$ multiplicative and supported on squarefree numbers,
$h(p)=\gamma'(p)-1$; so $h(p)\leq7$ for $p\leq W$ and $h(\ell)\leq2\ell^{-1/2}$ for
$\ell>W\geq16$.  Let $\cP_y$ be the set of prime powers $\leq y$, and for
$m\geq1$ put
\[
 Z_y(m)=\frac1{\log y}\sum_{d\in\cP_y,\ d\mid m}\Lambda(d),\qquad
 S_y(m)=\prod_{p\mid m,\ p^{v_p(m)}>y}p^{v_p(m)}.
\]
The \emph{local weight} $Z_y$ has bounded mean, since
$\sum_{p\leq y}\log p/(p\log y)\to1$, unlike $\Omega(m)$ or Rankin's $m^\eta$; and it
is large on smooth numbers.

\begin{lemma}[smooth numbers have a large local weight; {\cite[Lemma~2.1, (2.1$'$)]{KA3}}; \lab{proved}]\label{lem:localweight}
Let $y\geq2$ and let $m$ be $y$-smooth.  Then $S_y(m)$ is squarefull and
$\log m\leq\log S_y(m)+Z_y(m)\log y$.  Hence, if $m\geq Y$ and $S_y(m)\leq T$, then
$Z_y(m)\log y\geq\log(Y/T)$.  In particular, for $K\geq y$, $u=\log K/\log y$,
$K<m\leq2K$ and every $k\geq1$,
\[
 \ind[P(m)\leq y]\leq\ind[P(m)\leq y,\ S_y(m)>K^{1/2}]+(2Z_y(m)/u)^k .
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
If $p\mid S_y(m)$ then $p\leq y<p^{v_p(m)}$, so $v_p(m)\geq2$.  If
$p^{v_p(m)}\leq y$, all $p^\nu$ with $\nu\leq v_p(m)$ lie in $\cP_y$, and
$v_p(m)\log p=\sum_{\nu\leq v_p(m)}\Lambda(p^\nu)$.  Summing over the primes of
$m$ gives the inequality.  For the last claim take $Y=K$, $T=K^{1/2}$: if
$S_y(m)\leq K^{1/2}$ then $Z_y(m)\geq u/2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[moments of the local weight; {\cite[Lemma~2.2]{KA3}}; \lab{proved}]\label{lem:Zmoments}
For each $k\geq1$ there is $C_k(W)$ such that for all $y\geq2$,
\[
 \sum_{d_1,\dots,d_k\in\cP_y}\Lambda(d_1)\cdots\Lambda(d_k)\,\frac{\Gamma(D)}D\leq C_k(W)(\log y)^k,\qquad D=\lcm(d_1,\dots,d_k).
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Group the indices by the prime of $d_i$: a set partition $\pi$ of
$\{1,\dots,k\}$ into blocks $b$, distinct primes $p_b\leq y$ and exponents
$\nu_i\geq1$.  Then $\Gamma(D)/D=\prod_b\gamma'(p_b)p_b^{-\max_{i\in b}\nu_i}$.  For a
block of size $m$,
$\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb N^m}p^{-\max\nu}\leq\sum_{t\geq1}mt^{m-1}p^{-t}\leq c_m/p$, as
$p^{-t}\leq p^{-1}2^{1-t}$.  Dropping distinctness, $\pi$ contributes at most
$\prod_bc_{|b|}(\log y)^{|b|-1}\sum_{p\leq y}\gamma'(p)\log p/p$, and by Mertens and
$\gamma'(p)\leq1+2p^{-1/2}$ for $p>W$ the last sum is $\leq c'(W)\log y$.  Sum over
the $\mathrm{Bell}(k)$ partitions.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[smooth $\cR(M)$ blocks decay; {\cite[Lemma~2.3]{KA3}}; \lab{proved}]\label{lem:smoothR}
Fix $k\geq1$.  There are $C_k(W)$ and $y_0(k,W)$ such that for $y\geq y_0$,
$K\geq y^{16k}$ and $u=\log K/\log y$,
\[
 \sum_{K<M\leq2K,\ M\equiv3\ (4),\ P(M)\leq y}\tau(A_M^2)\Gamma(M)\leq C_k(W)\,K(\log K)^2u^{-k}.
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
By Lemma~\ref{lem:localweight} the sum is $\leq\Sigma_1+(2/u)^k\Sigma_2$, where
$\Sigma_1$ runs over the $y$-smooth $M\in(K,2K]$ with $S_y(M)>K^{1/2}$ (smoothness
is kept here, since otherwise $S_y(M)$ need not be squarefull), and
$\Sigma_2=\sum_{K<M\leq2K,\,M\equiv3\,(4)}\tau(A_M^2)\Gamma(M)Z_y(M)^k$ drops smoothness.

\emph{$\Sigma_1$.}  Pointwise $\tau(A_M^2)\Gamma(M)\leq8\tau(A_M)^23^{\omega(M)}\leq C_\epsilon K^\epsilon$.
There are $\ll t^{1/2}$ squarefull numbers $\leq t$, so
$\sum_{s\ \text{squarefull}>Z}1/s\ll Z^{-1/2}$, and the number of $y$-smooth
$M\leq2K$ with $S_y(M)>K^{1/2}$ is $\leq\sum_{s\ \text{squarefull}>K^{1/2}}2K/s\ll K^{3/4}$.
So $\Sigma_1\ll K^{4/5}$.

\emph{$\Sigma_2$.}  Expanding $Z_y^k$ and $\Gamma=1*h$,
\[
 \Sigma_2=\frac1{(\log y)^k}\sum_{d_1,\dots,d_k\in\cP_y}\prod_i\Lambda(d_i)\sum_eh(e)\,T(\lcm(D,e)),
\]
where $T(q)=\sum_{K<M\leq2K,\ M\equiv3\,(4),\ q\mid M}\tau(A_M^2)$.
$T(q)=0$ for even $q$.  For odd $q$, $q\mid M$ if and only if
$A_M\equiv4^{-1}\pmod q$, a reduced class, with $A$ in
$((K+1)/4,(2K+1)/4]$.  For $2\leq q\leq K^{1/2}$ Shiu's theorem (as stated at
the start of Section~\ref{sec:profiles}, with $F(A)=\tau(A^2)$, $F(p)=3$,
$F(p^a)=2a+1\leq3^a$, $V=(2K+1)/4$, $Y=K/4$, $\alpha_s=\beta_s=1/4$) gives
\[
 T(q)\ll\frac K{\varphi(q)\log K}\exp\Bigl\{\sum_{p\leq K,\ p\nmid q}\frac3p\Bigr\}
 \ll\frac Kq(\log K)^2\frac q{\varphi(q)}\prod_{p\mid q}\e^{-3/p}\leq C_S\frac Kq(\log K)^2,
\]
since $(1-1/p)^{-1}\e^{-3/p}\leq1$.  For $q=1$,
$\sum_{A\leq x}\tau(A^2)\ll x(\log x)^2$.  For every $q$,
$T(q)\leq3C_\epsilon K^{1+\epsilon}/q$.  Now $D\leq y^k\leq K^{1/16}$, so
$\lcm(D,e)\leq K^{1/2}$ when $e\leq K^{1/4}$, and
\[
 \sum_{e\leq K^{1/4}}\frac{h(e)}{\lcm(D,e)}\leq\frac1D\prod_{p\mid D}(1+h(p))\prod_{p\nmid D}\Bigl(1+\frac{h(p)}p\Bigr)\leq c_1(W)\frac{\Gamma(D)}D,
\]
while, by Rankin with exponent $1/4$ and $\omega(D)\leq k$,
\[
 \sum_{e>Z}\frac{h(e)\gcd(D,e)}e\leq Z^{-1/4}\prod_{p\mid D}(1+h(p)p^{1/4})\prod_{p\nmid D}(1+h(p)p^{-3/4})\leq c_2(W)2^kZ^{-1/4}.
\]
So $\sum_eh(e)T(\lcm(D,e))\leq c_3(W)[\Gamma(D)K(\log K)^2+2^kK^{1-1/16+\epsilon}]/D$, and
Lemma~\ref{lem:Zmoments} (with $\Gamma\geq1$ for the second term) gives
$\Sigma_2\leq C_k(W)[K(\log K)^2+K^{1-1/20}]$.  Finally
$K^{4/5}+K^{1-1/20}\leq K(\log K)^2u^{-k}$ for $K\geq K_0(k)$, which holds for
$y\geq y_0(k,W)$ since $u\leq\log K$.
\end{proof}

For Case A we use three results of \cite[\S7]{ElsholtzTao}, the section
from which its Prop.~1.4 (used in Lemma~\ref{lem:caseAprofile}) is
derived.  They were checked against the source but not re-proved.
\begin{itemize}
\item \cite[Cor.~7.4]{ElsholtzTao}: for natural numbers $a,b,N$ with
  $a,b\leq N^l$, $\sum_{n\leq N}\tau(an+b)\ll_l\tau(\gcd(a,b))N\log N$.
\item \cite[Thm~7.1]{ElsholtzTao}: if $P$ has degree $D$, nonnegative
  integer coefficients $\leq N^l$, and $\rho_P(p^j)\leq C$ for all prime powers,
  then $\sum_{n\leq N}\tau(P(n))\ll_{D,l,C}N\sum_{m\leq N}\rho_P(m)/m$.
\item \cite[(7.10)]{ElsholtzTao}: for $A,B\geq2$ and \emph{every} positive
  integer $k$,
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:ET710}
   \sum_{a\leq A}\sum_{m\leq B}\frac{\rho_{ka}(m)}m\ll A\log B\cdot\log(1+k),
  \end{equation}
  where $\rho_{ka}(m)=\#\{b\bmod m:\ kab^2+1\equiv0\ (m)\}$.
\end{itemize}
\begin{remark}[uniformity of \eqref{eq:ET710} in $k$, and two slips; {\cite[\S3]{KA3}}, {\cite[uniformity check, steps~3, 5]{KA3rev}}]\label{rem:ETslips}
In \cite{ElsholtzTao} the condition $k\ll(AB)^{O(1)}$ of Prop.~1.4 enters
only through Thm~7.1.  The proof of \eqref{eq:ET710}
\cite[pp.~30--32]{ElsholtzTao}, which splits into the ranges $q<A$,
$A\leq q\leq kA$ and $q>kA$ after quadratic reciprocity, uses no size
condition on $k$; so \eqref{eq:ET710} holds uniformly in $k$, which is how
we use it.  Two slips in that proof were found and are harmless.
(i) On p.~31 the character $a\mapsto(\frac{-ka}q)$ is said to have mean zero
for every $q$ in the small range; this fails for square $q$ (including
$q=1$).  Square $q$ contribute at most $A\log B\sum_{q\ \text{square}}1/q=O(A\log B)$,
so \eqref{eq:ET710} holds as stated.  (ii) In the range $q>kA$ the factor
$c(q)$ omits the reciprocity sign $(-1)^{\frac{q-1}2\cdot\frac{k'a-1}2}$.  For fixed $a$
the corrected factor is still $8$-periodic in $q$, and $q\mapsto(\frac{-ka}q)$ is
the Kronecker character of the negative number $-ka$, hence never
principal (even when $k'a$ is a square); so the mean-zero and partial
summation step holds with an absolute constant, uniformly in $k$.
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[smooth Case-A blocks decay; {\cite[Lemma~3.1]{KA3}}; \lab{proved mod} Elsholtz--Tao Thm~7.1, Cor.~7.4 and (7.10)]\label{lem:smoothA}
Fix $k\geq1$.  There are $C_k(W)$ and $y_0(k,W)$ such that for $y\geq y_0$,
$K\geq y^{64k}$ and $u=\log K/\log y$,
\[
 \sum_{r,h\geq1,\ K<rh\leq2K,\ P(rh)\leq y}\tau(4rh^2+1)\Gamma(4rh)\leq C_k(W)\,K(\log K)^2u^{-k}.
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
$\Gamma(4rh)\leq8\Gamma(r)\Gamma(h)$, and pointwise
$\tau(4rh^2+1)\Gamma(4rh)\leq C_\epsilon K^\epsilon$.  We split the pairs by the size of~$r$.

\emph{(i) $r\geq K^{1/4}$.}  Then $h\leq2K^{3/4}$ and $r$ is $y$-smooth, so by
Lemma~\ref{lem:localweight} with $(Y,T)=(K^{1/4},K^{1/8})$,
$1\leq\ind[S_y(r)>K^{1/8}]+(8Z_y(r)/u)^k$.  The pairs with $r$ $y$-smooth and
$S_y(r)>K^{1/8}$ number $\leq\sum_{h\leq2K}\sum_{s\ \text{squarefull}>K^{1/8}}2K/(hs)\ll K^{1-1/16}\log K$,
and contribute $\ll K^{1-1/20}$.  The moment part, with smoothness and the
lower bound $rh>K$ dropped, is at most
\begin{multline*}
 8\Bigl(\frac8u\Bigr)^k\frac1{(\log y)^k}\sum_{d_1,\dots,d_k\in\cP_y}\prod_i\Lambda(d_i)\sum_{s,t}h(s)h(t)\\
 \times\sum_{\substack{h\leq2K^{3/4}\\ t\mid h}}\ \sum_{\substack{r\leq2K/h\\ L\mid r}}\tau(4h^2r+1),\qquad L=\lcm(D,s).
\end{multline*}
For $s\leq K^{1/16}$ we have $D\leq K^{1/64}$, $L\leq K^{1/8}$; write $r=Lr'$ with
$r'\leq R'=2K/(hL)$, so $R'\geq K^{1/8}$.  The linear form $ar'+1$, with
$a=4h^2L\leq16K^{13/8}\leq R'^{14}$, is coprime, and Cor.~7.4 ($l=14$) gives
$\ll R'\log R'\leq2K\log K/(hL)$.  Summing over $h=th'$ gives a factor
$\leq2\log K/t$.  These terms total
$\ll K(\log K)^2(h(t)/t)(h(s)/\lcm(D,s))$, and $\sum_th(t)/t\leq c(W)$,
$\sum_sh(s)/\lcm(D,s)\leq c_1(W)\Gamma(D)/D$ as in Lemma~\ref{lem:smoothR}.  For
$s>K^{1/16}$ the inner sum is bounded trivially by $(2K/(hL))C_\epsilon K^\epsilon$, and the
Rankin tail of Lemma~\ref{lem:smoothR} gives $\ll2^kK^{1+\epsilon-1/64}\log K/D$.
Lemma~\ref{lem:Zmoments} bounds part~(i) by $C_k(W)[K(\log K)^2u^{-k}+K^{1-1/80}]$.

\emph{(ii) $r<K^{1/4}$.}  Then $h>K^{3/4}$ is $y$-smooth and, with
$(Y,T)=(K^{3/4},K^{1/4})$, $1\leq\ind[S_y(h)>K^{1/4}]+(2Z_y(h)/u)^k$.  The first
term contributes $\ll K^{1-1/20}$ as in~(i).  The moment part is at most
\begin{multline*}
 8\Bigl(\frac2u\Bigr)^k\frac1{(\log y)^k}\sum_{d_1,\dots,d_k\in\cP_y}\prod_i\Lambda(d_i)\sum_{s,t}h(s)h(t)\\
 \times\sum_{\substack{r<K^{1/4}\\ s\mid r}}\ \sum_{h'\leq2K/(rL)}\tau(4rL^2h'^2+1),\qquad L=\lcm(D,t).
\end{multline*}
Take $t\leq K^{1/16}$, so $L\leq K^{1/8}$.  Fix $r$, and put $P(n)=4rL^2n^2+1$,
$N=\lfloor2K/(rL)\rfloor\geq K^{1/2}$; its coefficients are $\leq4K^{1/2}\leq N^2$.  For
$p\mid2rL$, $P\equiv1\pmod p$, so $\rho_P(p^j)=0$.  For odd $p\nmid rL$, $n\mapsto Ln$
is a bijection modulo $p^j$ carrying the roots of $P$ to those of $4rb^2+1$,
so $\rho_P(p^j)=\rho_{4r}(p^j)\leq2$ by Hensel ($P'(n)=8rL^2n$ is a unit at a root).
By CRT $\rho_P(m)\leq\rho_{4r}(m)$ for all $m$.  Thm~7.1 (degree $2$, $l=2$,
$C=2$) gives $\sum_{n\leq N}\tau(P(n))\ll(2K/(rL))\sum_{m\leq2K}\rho_{4r}(m)/m$.  Write
$r=sa$, so $\rho_{4r}=\rho_{ka}$ with $k=4s$, and split $a\in[R,2R)$ dyadically
($\leq\log K$ ranges).  By \eqref{eq:ET710}, uniformly in $k=4s$, each range
gives $\ll(2K/(sRL))\cdot2R\log(2K)\log(1+4s)$.  These terms total
$\ll K(\log K)^2(h(s)\log(1+4s)/s)(h(t)/\lcm(D,t))$, and
$\sum_sh(s)\log(1+4s)/s\leq c(W)$ since $\sum_sh(s)s^{-3/4}<\infty$.  The terms
$t>K^{1/16}$ are bounded trivially as in~(i), and Lemma~\ref{lem:Zmoments}
bounds part~(ii) by $C_k(W)[K(\log K)^2u^{-k}+K^{1-1/80}]$.

Finally $K^{1-1/80}\leq K(\log K)^2u^{-k}$ for $K\geq K_0(k)$.
\end{proof}
In part~(ii) the multiplier $k=4s$ of \eqref{eq:ET710} is a divisor of $r$,
of size up to $K^{1/4}$; this is why uniformity in $k$ matters.

\begin{lemma}[first moments; {\cite[Cors.~2.4, 3.2, 3.3]{KA3}}, {\cite[Lemmas~3.3, 3.3$'$]{KA2}}; \lab{proved}; Case A \lab{proved mod} Elsholtz--Tao Prop.~1.4, Thm~7.1, Cor.~7.4, (7.10)]\label{lem:firstnoB}
For $y\geq y_0(W)$,
\[
 \mathfrak M(y)\leq K_3'(W)(\log y)^3.
\]
More precisely, the $\cR(M)$-part is $\leq K_R(W)(\log y)^3$, the Case-A part
is $\leq K_A(W)(\log y)^3$, the $(a,D)$-part is $\ll_W(\log y)^3$ and the selector
part is $\leq\log\log y+O(1)$.  If every modulus satisfies $G\leq P(G)^{1+B}$, the
sum over the classes of $\cG$ with $P(G)\leq y$ is $\leq C(W,B)(\log y)^3$, and
for Case A only Prop.~1.4 is used.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\emph{$\cR(M)$.}  Take $k=4$ and $X=y^{64}$.  On the body $M\leq2X$ drop the
smoothness condition; the weighted Shiu argument of
Lemma~\ref{lem:kmoments}(2), which uses no $B$, gives $\leq c(W)(\log2X)^3\leq
c'(W)(\log y)^3$.  The blocks $(K,2K]$, $K=2^t\geq X$, cover $M>2X$; by
Lemma~\ref{lem:smoothR} the block contributes
$\leq K^{-1}C_4K(\log2K)^2u_t^{-4}\leq4C_4(\log y)^2u_t^{-2}$, $u_t=t\log2/\log y$.
Summing over $t\geq t_0=\lceil\log X/\log2\rceil$ gives
$\leq4C_4(\log y)^4(\log2)^{-2}\sum_{t\geq t_0}t^{-2}\leq(C_4/(8\log2))(\log y)^3$.
\emph{Case A.}  Since the Case-A classes with $g(d)=rh$ are counted by
$\tau(4rh^2+1)$, the Case-A part is at most
$\sum_{P(rh)\leq y}\tau(4rh^2+1)\Gamma(4rh)/(4rh)$.  Take $k=4$ and $X=y^{256}$.
The body $rh\leq2X$ is Lemma~\ref{lem:caseAprofile} with its $\gamma$-weight
clause and partial summation, $\leq C(W)(\log2X)^3$; this uses Prop.~1.4.
The tail is summed by Lemma~\ref{lem:smoothA} exactly as for $\cR(M)$.
\emph{$(a,D)$ and selectors.}  The classes are counted by $(a,g(D))$ with
multiplicity at most $2^{\omega(g)}$, so the $(a,D)$-sum is at most
$2\sum_{P(a)\leq y}\Gamma(a)/a\cdot\sum_{P(g)\leq y}2^{\omega(g)}\Gamma(g)/g$, an Euler
product of size $\ll(\log y)^{1+2}$; the selector sum is
$\sum_{W<p\leq y}\gamma'(p)/p$ \cite[Lemmas~3.3, 3.3$'$]{KA2}.
\emph{Bounded $B$.}  Drop smoothness and sum over $G\leq y^{1+B}$, using the
body bounds only \cite[\S3]{KA2}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[where the previous version lost $(\log\log y)^3$; {\cite[\S1]{KA3}}; \emph{Assessment}]\label{rem:rankinloss}
The previous version of this note (following \cite[\S3]{KA2}) dropped
smoothness on $M\leq y^{u_0}$ and used Rankin's trick, with Cauchy--Schwarz
against a shifted-divisor moment, beyond.  Rankin bounds a dyadic block of
$y$-smooth moduli by $\e^{-u}$ times the \emph{whole} smooth sum, which is
$\asymp\log y$, so each block loses a full power of $\log y$; this forces
$u_0\asymp\log\log y$ and a mass $(\log y)^3(\log\log y)^3$.  H\"older in place of
Cauchy--Schwarz loses in the same way.  The local weight avoids this:
expanding $Z_y^4$ only imposes a divisibility $D\mid M$ with $D\leq y^4$, a
congruence to a \emph{small} modulus, to which Shiu's theorem
(resp.\ Elsholtz--Tao \S7) applies with no loss; the decay $u^{-4}$ is crude
(the truth is like $\rho(u)$) but beats the $u^2$ growth of the block masses.
Numerically the smooth first moments are $\asymp(\log y)^3$ up to $10^{12}$,
with block profile $\max_uu^4b(u)=0.385$ \cite[\S8]{KA3} \lab{evidence}.
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[second moment and leak; {\cite[Lemmas~4.1--4.3]{KA2}}; \lab{proved}, no external input]\label{lem:secondnoB}
For every prime $\ell>W$ and every family $\cG\subseteq\mathfrak U$,
$\E_{Q'}p_\ell^2\leq C(W)\ell^{-7/4}(\log\ell)^c$, with $C(W)\leq C(\log W)^c$
and $c$ absolute.  Hence there is an absolute $W_0$ such that for
$W\geq W_0$ and the block structure of Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap} below, the
leak satisfies $\mathfrak L\leq1/2$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
For classes with top prime power $\ell^v$ and cofactor $q$, so $G=q\ell^v$
with $P(q)<\ell$, one has $p_\ell\leq\sum_v\ell^{-v}N_{\ell,v}$, where
$N_{\ell,v}$ counts the active classes.  Expanding $\E N_{\ell,v}^2$ and
replacing the pointwise divisor bound by
$\mu(q)\mu(q')\leq(\mu(q)^2+\mu(q')^2)/2$ gives
$\E N_{\ell,v}^2\ll_W\log\ell\sum_{P(q)<\ell}\mu_{\ell,v}(q)^2\tau(q)\Gamma(q)/q$, with no
condition on the size of $q$.  For each type this cofactor sum is
$\ll_\kappa(v+1)^c[(\log\ell)^c+\ell^{\kappa v}]$: long dyadic blocks of $q$ by
Cauchy--Schwarz, Rankin and Shiu's theorem (resp.\ the Case-A box
moments), short blocks by pointwise divisor bounds; selector classes
contribute $1$.  With $\kappa=1/4$, Minkowski over $v$ and types gives the
bound, since $\sum_v\ell^{-7v/8}$ converges.  The leak is then
$\leq\sum_{\ell>W}\ell^{1/2}\E p_\ell^2\ll W^{-1/4}(\log W)^{3c+2}$, as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:kmoments}(4).  This lemma uses no external input, Case A
included.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The cap}
\begin{theorem}[the $3/4$ cap without $B$; {\cite[Thm~4.1]{KA3}}, improving {\cite[Thm~5.1]{KA2}}; \lab{proved}; Case A \lab{proved mod} Elsholtz--Tao Prop.~1.4, Thm~7.1, Cor.~7.4, (7.10)]\label{thm:noBcap}
There are absolute constants $W,\lambda_0,C$ such that the following
holds.  Let $\cG$ be any finite family of $\cR(M)$-, $(a,D)$-, Case-A and
selector classes, mixed arbitrarily, with arbitrary moduli, and let $\nu$
be a majorant of level $\lambda\geq\lambda_0$ of the whole avoider set
$\A(\cG)\subseteq\Z$: $\nu=\sum_ia_i\ind[n\equiv b_i\pmod{d_i}]\geq0$ on $\Z$,
$\nu\geq1$ on $\A(\cG)$, and $\sum_{\ell\mid d_i,\,\ell>W}\log\ell\leq\lambda$ for
every $i$.  Then
\[
 \log\frac1{\E\nu}\leq C\lambda^{3/4}.
\]
If $\cG$ contains no Case-A classes, the only external input is Shiu's
theorem.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:wseq} with the base $R_W^\square$ of
Lemma~\ref{lem:squarebase}, whose $R$-term is $\leq2W$ and which avoids
every $W$-smooth class of $\cG$; selector classes $0\bmod p$ with $p>W$
are ordinary classes decided at $p$.  The blocks are those of
Theorem~\ref{thm:karycap}, with singletons $W<\ell\leq\e^{s_1}$,
$s_1=\lambda^{1/4}$; the leak is $\leq1/2$ by
Lemma~\ref{lem:secondnoB}, and $W$ is fixed by it, so $K_3'=K_3'(W)$ of
Lemma~\ref{lem:firstnoB} is absolute.  The singletons cost
$\leq\frac83\mathfrak M(\e^{s_1})\leq\frac83K_3's_1^3=\frac83K_3'\lambda^{3/4}$.
For the block $V_i$, $s=2^is_1$, $d_i=\lfloor\lambda/s\rfloor$,
Corollary~\ref{cor:kmean} and Jensen give, as in
Theorem~\ref{thm:karycap},
$\E\Phi_i\leq d_i\log(C_0(\E m_{V_i}+4d_i)/d_i)+\frac43d_i+O(\log\lambda)$, and now
$\E m_{V_i}\leq\mathfrak M(\e^{2s})\leq8K_3's^3$, so
$(\E m_{V_i}+4d_i)/d_i\leq16K_3's^4/\lambda+4=16K_3'\cdot16^i+4$, and
$\E\Phi_i\leq(\lambda/(2^is_1))(c_1+i\log16)+O(\log\lambda)$.  Hence
$2\sum_i\E\Phi_i\leq C\lambda/s_1+O(\log^2\lambda)=C\lambda^{3/4}+O(\log^2\lambda)$.
The linear block $(\e^{\lambda/2},\e^\lambda]$ costs $2\log(1+3\e^{-\lambda/4})$, and primes
above $\e^\lambda$ cost nothing.  The constant $2W+\log2$ is absorbed into $C$
for $\lambda\geq\lambda_0$.
\end{proof}
This is the proof of the previous version \cite[Thm~5.1]{KA2} verbatim,
with the lossless first moment of Lemma~\ref{lem:firstnoB} in place of
$\mathfrak M(y)\ll(\log y)^3(\log\log y)^3$.  The previous version had to shrink
the singleton range to $s_1=\lambda^{1/4}(\log\lambda)^{-3/4}$ to balance the
singletons against the blocks, which gave $\lambda^{3/4}(\log\lambda)^{3/4}$; with
the lossless moment the balance is at $s_1=\lambda^{1/4}$ as in
Theorem~\ref{thm:karycap}.  The constants are absolute but astronomically
large; the second review of \cite{KA2} estimates $\log W\approx10^{10}$--$10^{11}$,
and \cite{KA3} does not change $W$.

\begin{theorem}[bounded $B$, all four types; {\cite[Thm~5.2]{KA2}}; \lab{proved}; Case A \lab{proved mod} Elsholtz--Tao Prop.~1.4]\label{thm:Bcapall}
Fix $B\geq0$.  If every modulus of $\cG$ satisfies $G\leq P(G)^{1+B}$
(automatic for selector classes), then in Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}
$\log(1/\E\nu)\leq C(B)\lambda^{3/4}$ for $\lambda\geq\lambda_0(B)$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
As Theorem~\ref{thm:karycap} ($s_1=\lambda^{1/4}$), with
Lemma~\ref{lem:squarebase} for the base, the bounded-$B$ clause of
Lemma~\ref{lem:firstnoB} for the first moments and
Lemma~\ref{lem:secondnoB} for the leak.
\end{proof}
So Theorem~\ref{thm:karycap} extends verbatim to $(a,D)$-, Case-A and
selector classes.  Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap} now implies it with a
constant independent of $B$; Theorem~\ref{thm:Bcapall} is kept because
its Case-A part needs only Prop.~1.4 of \cite{ElsholtzTao}, not
\cite[\S7]{ElsholtzTao}.  Nowhere in this section does the number of
prime factors of a modulus enter; this is why the $k$-ary route has no
analogue of the middle window $r\asymp\log L$ of the $\Lambda^2$ route
(Section~\ref{sec:selberg}).

\subsection{The main theorem}
\begin{theorem}[no $\theta>3/4$ for coefficient-sum CRT sieves; {\cite[Cor.~4.2]{KA3}}, improving {\cite[Cor.~6.1]{KA2}}; \lab{proved}; Case A \lab{proved mod} Elsholtz--Tao Prop.~1.4, Thm~7.1, Cor.~7.4, (7.10)]\label{thm:main}
% keep in sync: es-threequarter-note.tex cites thm:main and rem:TQcovered by number
Fix $A\geq1$.  Let a method bound $\#(\A(\cG)\cap[1,N])$ by
$N\cdot\E\nu+\sum_i|a_i|$, where
\begin{itemize}
\item $\cG$ is any finite family of $\cR(M)$-, $(a,D)$-, Case-A and selector
  classes (Definition~\ref{def:types}; arbitrary moduli, no $B$, no dominant
  prime, any number and multiplicity of prime factors);
\item $\nu=\sum_ia_i\ind[n\equiv b_i\pmod{d_i}]$ is a CRT majorant of any
  level, $\geq0$ on $\Z$ and $\geq1$ on all of $\A(\cG)\subseteq\Z$;
\item every prime dividing a modulus of $\cG$ is at most $N^A$;
\item $\sum_i|a_i|<N$.
\end{itemize}
Then the saving $s=\log(N/\text{bound})$ satisfies
\[
 s\leq C_A(\log N)^{3/4}
\]
for $N\geq N_0(A)$.  In particular no method of this class proves
$E(N)\ll N\exp\{-(\log N)^\theta\}$ with $\theta>3/4$, nor
$E(N)\leq N\exp\{-(\log N)^{3/4}\omega(N)\}$ with $\omega(N)\to\infty$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\emph{Projection.}  Let $Q$ be the least common multiple of the moduli of
$\cG$; $\A(\cG)$ is $Q$-periodic.  Replace $\nu$ by its conditional
average $\bar\nu(n)=\E[\nu(n')\mid n'\equiv n\pmod Q]$.  A term
$a\ind[n\equiv b\pmod d]$ becomes $a(d'/d)\ind[n\equiv b\pmod{d'}]$ with
$d'=(d,Q)$, or vanishes.  So $\bar\nu\geq0$, $\bar\nu\geq1$ on $\A(\cG)$,
$\E\bar\nu=\E\nu$, $\sum|\bar a_i|\leq\sum|a_i|<N$, and every prime of every
modulus of $\bar\nu$ is a family prime, hence $\leq N^A$.
\emph{Coarsening.}  Put $S=\log(1/\E\nu)$; since the bound is $\geq N\E\nu$,
$s\leq S$.  Lemma~\ref{lem:budget}, whose proof uses only
$\log\ell\leq\Lambda_0$ for every charged prime (here every prime $>W$),
with $\Lambda_0=A\log N$ and $\lambda=(A+2)\log N$, gives a majorant
$\nu'\geq\bar\nu$ of $\A(\cG)$ of level $\leq\lambda$ with
$\E\nu'\leq\E\nu+N^{-1}$, as in Corollary~\ref{cor:budgetlevel}.  So
$\log(1/\E\nu')\geq\min(S,\log N)-\log2$, and Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}
at level $\lambda$ gives
$\min(S,\log N)\leq\log2+C\lambda^{3/4}=\log2+C(A+2)^{3/4}(\log N)^{3/4}$.  For $N\geq N_0$ the right side is $<\log N$,
so the minimum is $S$, and $s\leq S$ is bounded as claimed.
\end{proof}
For whole-avoider architectures, Theorem~\ref{thm:main} subsumes the
previous form of the main theorem: case (i), families whose moduli have
a dominant prime (Corollary~\ref{cor:dominant}, whose $R$ is a selector
together with family classes), and case (ii), $\cR(M)$-families with
$M\leq P(M)^{1+B}$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:karycap}).  Case (ii) is subsumed only
for majorants that are $\geq1$ on the whole avoider set.
Theorem~\ref{thm:karycap} also applies to majorants that are $\geq1$ only
on the avoiders in the quadratic-residue set $R_W$; that form is not
implied by Theorem~\ref{thm:main} and remains a separate statement.  The
square base $R_W^\square$ is a proof device: Theorem~\ref{thm:main} needs
no admissible set.  A selector $R=\{c:(c,P)=1\}$ in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:arch} is the same as adding the selector classes
$0\bmod p$, $p\mid P$, to $\cG$.  So every CRT-majorant architecture
(Definition~\ref{def:arch}) for such a family, with a selector
admissible set and with all primes of the family (selector primes and
small parts of moduli included) $\leq N^A$, saves at most
$C_A(\log N)^{3/4}$.  The previous version of this note proved
$C_A(\log N)^{3/4}(\log\log N)^{3/4}$ in general and $C_{A,B}(\log N)^{3/4}$
only under bounded $B$; the gap, gains $(\log N)^{3/4}\omega(N)$ with
$1\ll\omega(N)\leq(\log\log N)^{3/4}$ for unbounded-$B$ families, is now closed
\cite[Cor.~4.2]{KA3}.  So, up to constants, the cap and the saving
$c(\log N)^{3/4}$ of \cite{TQ} agree for every family of the four types.

\begin{remark}[the $3/4$ note's majorant is literally covered; {\cite[Remark~5.4]{KA2}}; \lab{proved}]\label{rem:TQcovered}
The majorant of \cite{TQ} is $\nu_X=S_y\,Q_r(H_X)$ with
$S_y=\ind[(n,P_y)=1]$ and $y>W$.  It is $\geq1$ only on $y$-rough avoiders,
so it is \emph{not} a majorant of $\A(\cG_0)$ for the atom family $\cG_0$
alone; it \emph{is} a majorant of $\A(\cG_0\cup\{0\bmod p:p\leq y\})$, since
$Q_r(H)=\binom{H-1}r\geq0$ for even $r$ and $Q_r(0)=1$.  The atoms
$-uv^{-1}\bmod k\ell$ are $\cR(k\ell)$-classes with $k\ell\leq\ell^{1+2\kappa}$,
$\kappa<1/240$ (checked in \cite[check~(3)]{KA2rev2} on $124{,}464$ atoms), so
this family lies in the class of Theorem~\ref{thm:Bcapall} with $B<1/120$.
The note's coefficient budget $T_{\rm abs}\leq N^{1/2}<N$ and its primes
$\leq X\leq N$ give the remaining hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:main}.
Hence its saving $c(\log N)^{3/4}$ matches the cap $C(\log N)^{3/4}$ in
order.  The constants cannot be compared: the note's are ineffective
(Bombieri--Vinogradov), and those here are astronomically large.  The
selector primes $p\leq W$ are absorbed by the base, and those in $(W,y]$
cost $O(\log\log y)$ in the singleton steps, the same order as the
selector term $\log(P_y/\varphi(P_y))$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:TQinclass}.  The
note's transfer from exceptional primes to $E(N)$ by the multiplicative
semigroup is non-CRT post-processing that only loses, so it does not
affect this reading.  This complements Lemma~\ref{lem:TQinclass}, which
places the same majorant in a prime-slice system.
\end{remark}

\emph{What the class contains} \cite[\S6]{KA2}, \cite[(C)]{KA2rev2}.
Nonnegative CRT majorants of the \emph{whole} avoider set of any finite
mixture of the four types, used with the coefficient-sum rounding bound.
This includes Bonferroni and inclusion--exclusion truncations, Selberg's
$\Lambda^2$ over any set system of these classes (its error
$\sum|\lambda_T\lambda_{T'}|$ is at least the merged $\sum|a_i|$), the $\beta$-
and Rosser sieves and any combinatorial upper-bound sieve on the class
system, CRT-evaluated moment and variance methods
(Section~\ref{sec:noncrt}), the majorant of the $3/4$ note
(Remark~\ref{rem:TQcovered}), and the sequential, $\Lambda^2$ and
Selberg-type majorants of Sections~\ref{sec:limit}--\ref{sec:selberg}
when they are $\geq1$ on the whole avoider set (several of them, such
as Theorems~\ref{thm:gapped} and~\ref{thm:karycap} and the $\Lambda^2$
majorants of \cite{TW2}, are stated for the avoiders in $R_W$ only; see
Remark~\ref{rem:L2vsmain}).
\emph{Not transferred} from the earlier sections: Montgomery's large sieve
is capped only fibrewise over prime slices (Remark~\ref{rem:LS}), not as a
pointwise majorant of a mixed family; and the per-frequency rounding of
Theorem~\ref{thm:perfreq} is proved for finite prime-slice families only.
Theorem~\ref{thm:main} needs the rounding $\sum|a_i|$.

\section{The campaign sieves lie in the class}\label{sec:campaign}

\subsection{The three-quarter note}
\begin{lemma}[{\cite[Lemma~4.1]{ET}}; \lab{proved}]\label{lem:TQinclass}
The atom family $\A_X$ of \cite[\S2]{TQ} is a prime-slice system with
$Q_0=\lcm(L_K,P_y)$, $\cP$ the primes in $(X^{1/2},X]$, the selector
$R=\{(c,P_y)=1\}$, $|F_\ell(c)|\leq\ell^{1/3}$, and
$\sum_\ell p_\ell(c)\ell^{-\alpha}\leq C_ut^3X^{-\alpha/2}$ for all $c$ (the
upper half of \cite[Cor.~4.3]{TQ}, ``uniform fibre masses''),
where $t=\log X$.  Its majorant $\nu_X=S_y\,Q_r(H_X)$ is $\geq0$ on $\Z$,
equals $1$ on the whole avoider set $\A$ (there $S_y=1$, $H_X=0$ and
$Q_r(0)=1$; \cite[item~3.1]{ETrev}; \cite[Thm~8.2]{TQ} itself states only
$\nu_X\geq1$ on exceptional primes), and has level $\lambda=rt$.  Hence every majorant on
this family has
\[
 \log\frac1{\E\nu}\leq\log\log y+\min\Bigl\{Ct^3,\ C\frac\lambda t\Bigl(1+\log^+\frac{Ct^4}\lambda\Bigr)\Bigr\}+O(\log^2\lambda).
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The structure is \cite[Lemma~2.2]{TQ} (deduplication and
conditional independence): conditional on $c\bmod L_K$ the coordinates
$n\bmod\ell$ are independent and uniform, and an atom $(k,\ell,u,v)$ forbids
$-uv^{-1}\bmod\ell$ in the fibres with $u+cv\equiv0\pmod k$; at most
$z_j^2\leq\ell^{1/3}$ atoms live at each $\ell$.  The majorant is a product of
the selector $S_y$ and $Q_r(H)=\binom{H-1}r$, both nonnegative, and $Q_r(0)=1$.
A term of $Q_r(H_X)$ involves at most $r$ atoms, hence at most $r$ slice
primes $\leq X$, so its level is at most $rt$.  The bound is
Theorem~\ref{thm:slice} with $\alpha$ optimised and the selector term
$\log(P_y/\varphi(P_y))=\log\log y+O(1)$.
\end{proof}
With $\lambda\leq A\log N$ the maximum over $t$ is $\asymp(\log N)^{3/4}$,
attained only for $t\asymp(\log N)^{1/4}$.  What forces $\lambda\lesssim\log
N$ is not the note's modulus bound, which it calls ``not necessary'', but
its coefficient budget $T_{\rm abs}\leq N^{1/2}$ \cite[proof of Thm~8.2]{TQ}:
the slice primes are $\leq X$, so $\Lambda_0=t\leq\log N$, and
Lemma~\ref{lem:budget} converts any majorant on this family with
coefficient sum $<N$ into one of level $\leq t+\log N+s$ with at most twice
the mean.  So the note satisfies (A1)--(A3), and \emph{its exponent $3/4$
is sharp for its own architecture} \lab{proved}.  This was the review's
one substantive correction \cite[item~3, SC1]{ETrev}.  Independently of
the prime-slice reading, the same majorant is a majorant of the whole
avoider set of the atom family together with the selector classes
$0\bmod p$, $p\leq y$, with $B<1/120$, so Theorems~\ref{thm:Bcapall}
and~\ref{thm:main} apply to it directly (Remark~\ref{rem:TQcovered}).

\begin{lemma}[Bonferroni depth; {\cite[Lemma~4.2]{ET}}; \lab{proved}]\label{lem:depth}
For even $r$ and any integer $H\geq0$, $\E Q_r(H)\geq\Prob(H\geq r+1)$.  If $H$
is a sum of independent Bernoulli variables with mean $\mu$ and
$r+1\leq\mu-2\sqrt\mu$, then $\E Q_r(H)\geq3/4$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
$Q_r(h)=\binom{h-1}r\geq1$ for $h\geq r+1$, and Chebyshev with $\mathrm{Var}\,H\leq\mu$.
\end{proof}
So the depth $r\gg t^3$ in \cite{TQ} is forced.  The binding constraint in
the $3/4$ proof is the pair (cubic supply $\mu_c\leq C_ut^3$, budget
$\log T_{\rm abs}\leq\log N$).  Bonferroni depth, the Bombieri--Vinogradov
level, the selector and the pruning cannot improve the exponent: the cap
of Theorem~\ref{thm:slice} holds for every majorant, and
Lemmas~\ref{lem:profileB}--\ref{lem:caseAprofile} bound the \emph{entire}
forced-class supply independently of any level of distribution, so
Elliott--Halberstam cannot raise the profile either \cite[Lemma~4.3 and
remark]{ET} \lab{proved}.  In the exchangeable \emph{binomial} model,
Selberg's square majorant attains the optimum up to a factor $e^{O(k)}$; this
is a theorem \cite[Thm.~1.1, Cor.~1.3]{PYY}.  For the Poisson model the
numerically computed linear-programming optimum agrees with the Selberg
value to within $0.01$ in $-\log$ in the tested cases \cite[\S2.5]{ET}.
Attainment for the multi-band families here is only \lab{evidence}.

\subsection{The two-thirds loglog note}
The note \cite{LL} is not a majorant sieve but Montgomery's large sieve
over progressions modulo $L_{\mathcal K}$, with multipliers
$\mathcal K=\{k\leq\delta\log N\}$.  It is covered through
Remark~\ref{rem:LS}.

\begin{corollary}[restricted multiplier sets; {\cite[Cor.~3.5]{ET}}; \lab{proved} given the notes' Brun--Titchmarsh upper bounds]\label{cor:restricted}
Suppose a family at scale $X=\e^t$ uses slice primes in $(X^{1/2},X]$ and
multipliers $k\in\mathcal K$, with $\sum_\ell p_\ell(c)\leq A_0t^2h(\mathcal K)$
for all $c$, where $h(\mathcal K)=\sum_{k\in\mathcal K}\varphi(k)/k^2$.  Then
\[
 \text{saving}\leq\min\Bigl\{Ct^2h,\ \frac{38\lambda}t\Bigl(1+\log^+\frac{Ct^3h}\lambda\Bigr)\Bigr\}+O(\log^2\lambda),
\]
and the supremum over $t$ is $\asymp\lambda^{2/3}h^{1/3}$.  For
$\mathcal K=\{k\leq\delta\log N\}$, $h\asymp\log\log N$, giving
$(\log N)^{2/3}(\log\log N)^{1/3}$; the same holds for any $\mathcal K$ with
$\lcm(\mathcal K)\leq N$, since then
$h(\mathcal K)\leq\prod_{p\mid\lcm}(1+1/p)\ll\log\log N$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Insert $\sum_\ell\bar p_\ell\ell^{-\alpha}\leq A_0t^2h\e^{-\alpha t/2}$ into
\eqref{eq:slice}.  With $t_0=(\lambda/h)^{1/3}$, for $t\leq t_0$ the first
entry is $\leq C\lambda^{2/3}h^{1/3}$; for $t=ut_0\geq t_0$ the second is
$\leq C\lambda^{2/3}h^{1/3}(1+3\log u)/u$.
\end{proof}
The note's large-sieve level is $Q^2\leq N/L_{\mathcal K}$, so
$\lambda\leq\log N$, and its admissible set is the reduced residues modulo
$L_{\mathcal K}$, with $R$-term
$\log(L_{\mathcal K}/\varphi(L_{\mathcal K}))\ll\log\log\log N$.
By Remark~\ref{rem:LS} and Corollary~\ref{cor:restricted} its saving is
at most $C(\log N)^{2/3}(\log\log N)^{1/3}$, i.e.\ its bound is never
smaller than $N\exp\{-C(\log N)^{2/3}(\log\log N)^{1/3}\}$: \emph{the $2/3$-loglog
note is sharp for its architecture}
\cite[Lemma~4.4]{ET}, \lab{proved}.
Vaughan's argument and that of Pomerance--Weingartner~\cite{PW} are the
case $\mathcal K=\{1\}$, giving $(\log N)^{2/3}$.  For the $3/4$ note,
$h\asymp\kappa t$ and the same computation gives $\lambda^{3/4}$.

\section{Selberg-type majorants}\label{sec:selberg}

\subsection{A sieve limit with no structural hypothesis}
Let $\Omega=\prod_i\Omega_i$ be a finite product probability space (CRT
coordinates), with costs $s_i\geq0$: $s_i=\log\ell$ for a slice prime and
$s_i=0$ for free coordinates.  Let $\A\subseteq\Omega$ be \emph{arbitrary}.
Let $V_\kappa$ be the span of functions of $\omega_T$ over sets $T$ with
$\sum_{i\in T}s_i\leq\kappa$; it is the sum of the Efron--Stein components
$H_T$ over these $T$.

\begin{theorem}[Selberg-type sieve limit; {\cite[Thm~5.5]{ET}}; \lab{proved}]\label{thm:L2}
Let $g\in V_{\lambda/2}$ with $g\geq1$ on $\A$, so that $\nu=g^2$ is a
majorant of level $\lambda$.  Then for every $\alpha>0$,
\[
 \E g^2\geq\e^{-\alpha\lambda/2}\,\frac{\Prob(\A)^2}{\Prob(\omega\in\A,\ \omega'\in\A)},
\]
where $\omega'$ is the $\rho$-correlated copy: independently for each $i$,
$\omega'_i=\omega_i$ with probability $\rho_i=\e^{-\alpha s_i}$ and otherwise a
fresh sample.  Equivalently, $\log(1/\E\nu)\leq\alpha\lambda/2+\Xi_\A(\alpha)$
with the \emph{noise-stability excess}
$\Xi_\A(\alpha)=\log[\Prob(\A\cap\A'_\alpha)/\Prob(\A)^2]\in[0,\log(1/\Prob(\A))]$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
$\E[g\ind_\A]\geq\Prob(\A)$ and, since $g\in V=V_{\lambda/2}$,
$\E[g\ind_\A]=\langle g,\Pi_V\ind_\A\rangle\leq\|g\|\,\|\Pi_V\ind_\A\|$.  Now
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \|\Pi_V\ind_\A\|^2&=\sum_{c(T)\leq\lambda/2}\|(\ind_\A)_T\|^2
 \leq\e^{\alpha\lambda/2}\sum_T\e^{-\alpha c(T)}\|(\ind_\A)_T\|^2\\
 &=\e^{\alpha\lambda/2}\langle\ind_\A,T_\rho\ind_\A\rangle
 =\e^{\alpha\lambda/2}\Prob(\A\cap\A'),
 \end{aligned}
\]
where $c(T)=\sum_{i\in T}s_i$ and
the noise operator $T_\rho$ acts on $H_T$ by $\prod_{i\in T}\rho_i$.
\end{proof}
This covers Selberg's $\Lambda^2$ sieve.  For a prime-slice system the
excess factorises fibrewise,
$\Xi_{\A_c}(\alpha)=\sum_\ell\log(1+\rho_\ell p_\ell(c)/(1-p_\ell(c)))
\leq\frac43\sum_\ell p_\ell(c)\ell^{-\alpha}$ when $p_\ell(c)\leq1/4$, so
Theorem~\ref{thm:L2} reproduces the Rankin functional \cite[Cor.~5.6]{ET}.
Its point is that balanced, twin and shared-prime moduli are allowed: for
$\Lambda^2$-majorants, beating $3/4$ with balanced moduli requires the
following hypothesis to fail.

\begin{hypothesis}[$H_{\rm MS}^{\rm Sel}$; {\cite[\S5.7]{ET}}; \lab{open}]\label{hyp:MSsel}
For the complete Case-B and Case-A forced-class system with moduli
$\leq\e^\lambda$, with primes $\leq\lambda^4$ given $\rho=1$,
$\Xi_\A(\alpha)\ll\alpha^{-3}(\log\lambda)^{O(1)}$ uniformly for
$\lambda^{-1}\leq\alpha\leq1$.
\end{hypothesis}
Under Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:MSsel}, Theorem~\ref{thm:L2} caps Selberg-type
majorants at $\lambda^{3/4}(\log\lambda)^{O(1)}$ for all moduli
\lab{conditional}.
A fibrewise version alone would not suffice: $\A=\{c=c_0\}\times\Omega'$
has $\Xi=0$ in every nonempty fibre, yet $g=\ind_{c=c_0}$ saves $\log Q_0$.
The remedy is to tilt the fibre law.

\begin{lemma}[fibre tilting; {\cite[Lemma~6.6]{TW}}, {\cite[Lemma~2.1]{TW2}}; \lab{proved}]\label{lem:tilt}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:L2}, let $F$ be a set of coordinates
whose residues $c$ (the fibre) range over $\Z/Q_F$; put
$\A_c=\A\cap\{\text{fibre}=c\}$ and
$\Xi_c(\alpha)=\log[\Prob(\A_c\cap\A'_c\mid c)/\Prob(\A_c\mid c)^2]$, where
in $\A'_c$ the coordinates outside $F$ are $\rho$-correlated and the fibre
is shared.  For any probability law $P$ on fibres with $\A_c\neq\emptyset$
on its support, every $g\in V_{\lambda/2}$ with $g\geq1$ on $\A$ satisfies
\[
 \log\frac1{\E g^2}\leq\frac{\alpha\lambda}2+\log\Bigl\|\frac{dP}{dU}\Bigr\|_\infty+\E_P\,\Xi_c(\alpha).
\]
For $P$ uniform on a set $R$ the middle term is $\log(Q_F/|R|)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}[Proof for uniform $P$ on $R$]
Let $\tilde\sigma=\sum_{c\in R}\pi_cU(\cdot\mid c,\A)$ and $h=d\tilde\sigma/dU$.
Then $\E_U[gh]\geq1$, so $\E g^2\geq1/\|\Pi_{V_{\lambda/2}}h\|^2$, and
$\|\Pi_Vh\|^2\leq\e^{\alpha\lambda/2}\langle h,T_\rho h\rangle$.  Since
$\langle h,T_\rho h\rangle$ is nondecreasing in each $\rho_i$, put
$\rho_i=1$ on $F$; then
$\langle h,T_\rho h\rangle=Q_F\sum_{c\in R}\pi_c^2\e^{\Xi_c}$.  Choosing
$\pi_c\propto\e^{-\Xi_c}$ and Jensen give $\leq(Q_F/|R|)\e^{\mathrm{avg}_R\Xi_c}$.
The general case is \cite[Lemma~2.1]{TW2}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Two large primes, twins included}
\emph{Setting} \cite[\S3]{TW2}.  Fix $B\geq0$, $A_0\geq1$; let $X$ be large,
$L=\log X$, $W_1=L^{1/2}$, $w_2=L^8$ and $\alpha=L^{-1/4}$.  The family is
any set of classes $-4D\pmod M$ of $\cR(M)$ with $M\leq X$,
$M\leq P(M)^{1+B}$, and \emph{at most two distinct prime factors of $M$
above $w_2$}.  Twin moduli $k\ell_1\ell_2$ ($\ell_i>w_2$, $k$ $w_2$-smooth),
dominant moduli and prime powers are included.  All primes are charged,
and the level is $\lambda\leq A_0L$.  The fibre is $c=n\bmod$ the
$w_2$-smooth part, with a law $P$ built from the QR base at primes
$\leq W_1$, conditioning on avoidance of the small classes, and a
good-fibre event; then $\log\|dP/dU\|_\infty\leq4L^{1/2}$
\cite[Lemmas~3.1--3.4]{TW2}.  In a fibre, a class $k\ell^v$ is unary at
$\ell$ and a class $k\ell^vm^u$ is a binary \emph{edge}.

The probabilistic core is a binary noise-stability bound.  Take
independent coordinates $y_\ell$ with laws $\nu_\ell$ and a finite set of
edges $e=\{(\ell,a),(m,c)\}$, $\ell\neq m$, hit when $y_\ell=a$ and
$y_m=c$.  Put $\pi_e=\nu_\ell(a)\nu_m(c)$,
$\deg(\ell,a)=\sum\nu_m(c)$ over the edges at $(\ell,a)$,
$w_\ell=\sum_{e\ni\ell}\pi_e$ and $q_\ell=\sum_a\nu_\ell(a)\deg(\ell,a)^2$,
and assume $w_\ell\leq\delta\leq1/16$ for all $\ell$.  Let $Z_1$ be the
probability that no edge is hit, and $Z_2(\tilde\rho)$ the probability that
no edge is hit in either copy of the coupled pair in which, independently
for each $\ell$, $y'_\ell=y_\ell$ with probability $\tilde\rho_\ell$ and is
otherwise an independent copy.

\begin{theorem}[binary noise stability; {\cite[Thm~1.4]{TW2}}; \lab{proved}]\label{thm:binaryNS}
For every $\tilde\rho\in[0,1]^{\text{index set}}$,
\[
 \log\frac{Z_2(\tilde\rho)}{Z_1^2}\leq(1+25\delta)\Bigl[\sum_{e=\{\ell,m\}}\tilde\rho_\ell\tilde\rho_m\pi_e+\sum_j\tilde\rho_jq_j\Bigr].
\]
\end{theorem}
The proof \cite[\S1]{TW2} differentiates $\log Z_2$ along $\kappa=t\tilde\rho$,
uses an identity for $\partial_{\kappa_j}\log Z_2$ under the unconditioned
law of the other coordinates, and bounds the result with a conditional
form of the Lov\'asz local lemma; no cluster expansion is needed.  An
exact check on 1200 small systems found no violation \lab{evidence}.

For a fibre $c$, a prime $j>w_2$ and a residue $a$, let $\deg_c(j,a)$ be
the partner mass of the active binary classes through $(j,a)$, and
$S_j(c)=\sum_a\nu_j(a)\min(\deg_c(j,a),1)^2$.

\begin{theorem}[two-prime $\Lambda^2$ cap, reduced; {\cite[Thm~5.1]{TW2}}; \lab{proved}]\label{thm:twoprime}
In this setting, for $L$ large, every $g\in V_{\lambda/2}$ with $g\geq1$ on
the avoiders of the family satisfies
\[
 \log\frac1{\E g^2}\leq\frac{A_0}2L^{3/4}+C_BL^{3/4}(\log L)^C
 +11\,\E_P\sum_{j>w_2}\rho_jS_j(c),\qquad\rho_j=j^{-\alpha},
\]
with an absolute exponent $C$.
\end{theorem}
The proof combines Lemma~\ref{lem:tilt} with the fibre law $P$,
Theorem~\ref{thm:binaryNS} in each good fibre after a free quarantine of
hub residues \cite[Lemma~2.2]{TW2}, and a profile bound $\ll\alpha^{-3}(\log
L)^{O(1)}$ for the unary and diagonal binary terms \cite[Lemma~4.1]{TW2}.

The off-diagonal term was left open in \cite{TW2}.  It is now bounded
unconditionally \cite{TW3}; the review \cite{TW3rev} found the argument
sound after repairs.

\begin{theorem}[the off-diagonal term $(H_O)$; {\cite[Thm~4.1]{TW3}}; \lab{proved}]\label{thm:HO}
In this setting,
$\E_P\sum_{j>w_2}\rho_jS_j(c)\ll_B\alpha^{-3}(\log L)^{O(1)}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
The prime-power classes are split off as in \cite[Lemma~5.4]{TW2}, at cost
$o(1)$.  For the classes with moduli $kjm$ ($j\neq m$ primes $>w_2$, $k$
$w_2$-smooth), call a class through $j$ \emph{small} if $m\leq(kj)^{C_0}$
and \emph{large} otherwise, for a fixed $C_0\geq6$, and write
$\deg_c(j,a)=x(a)+z(a)$ accordingly.  Since $\min(x+z,1)\leq\min(x,1)+z$ and
$\min(x,1)^2\leq x$,
\[
 S_j\leq2w_j^{\rm sm}(c)+2\sum_a\nu_j(a)z(a)^2,
\]
where $w_j^{\rm sm}$ is the binary mass at $j$ of the small active classes.
\emph{Small part} \cite[Lemma~2.1]{TW3}: by Shiu's theorem along the top
prime, the divisor mass of a small class is $(\log kj)^3$ rather than
$L^3$, and $\sum_j\rho_j(\log j)^3/j\ll\alpha^{-3}$; so its first moment is
$\ll\alpha^{-3}(\log L)^{O(1)}$.  \emph{Large part}
\cite[Lemmas~3.1--3.4]{TW3}: parametrise $D\mid A^2$ by $A=uvt$, $(u,v)=1$,
$D=u^2t$.  The largest of $u,v,t$ is at least $A^{1/3}$, and $m>(kj)^{C_0}$
makes the range of the prime $m$ long enough for Brun--Titchmarsh in a
class modulo four times the product of the two shorter variables.  The
residue modulo $j$ depends only on those two variables, and their second
moments give $\sum_{a\bmod j}V_{j,k}(a)^2\ll(\log L)^6$ uniformly in $j,k$.
Activity and the $k$-sum are handled as in \cite[Lemma~5.4]{TW2}.  The
large part contributes $\ll(\log L)^{O(1)}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[two-prime $\Lambda^2$ cap, twins included; {\cite[Cor.~5.2]{TW2}}, {\cite[Cor.~4.2]{TW3}}; \lab{proved}]\label{thm:twoprimecap}
Fix $B\geq0$ and $A_0\geq1$.  Let the family consist of classes of
$\cR(M)$ with $M\leq X$, $M\leq P(M)^{1+B}$, and at most two distinct prime
factors of $M$ above $(\log X)^8$ (twin moduli $k\ell_1\ell_2$, dominant
moduli and prime powers included).  Then every $g\in V_{\lambda/2}$,
$\lambda\leq A_0\log X$, with $g\geq1$ on the avoiders of the family
satisfies
\[
 \log\frac1{\E g^2}\ll_{A_0,B}L^{3/4}(\log L)^{O(1)},\qquad L=\log X.
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Theorems~\ref{thm:twoprime} and~\ref{thm:HO}.
\end{proof}

\emph{Scope.}  This is a statement about Selberg-type majorants $g^2$
only.  It needs at most two prime factors above $(\log X)^8$; the
extension to any fixed number is Theorem~\ref{thm:rprime} below.  The
restriction $M\leq P(M)^{1+B}$ with $B$ fixed excludes, for fixed $B$,
twins $k\ell_1\ell_2$ with a $w_2$-smooth cofactor $k>M^{(B-1)/(B+1)}$; for
a fixed number of large primes it is removed by Theorem~\ref{thm:noB}.
The bound is stated in $L=\log X$, with moduli $\leq X$ and level
$\lambda\leq A_0L$.  The level hypothesis $\lambda\leq A_0L$ is assumed
directly: the coarsening of Lemma~\ref{lem:budget} does not preserve the
square form $g^2$, so the reduction from a coefficient budget to a level
(Corollary~\ref{cor:budgetlevel}) is not available here as stated
(observation of this note, not from \cite{TW2,TW3}).

\begin{remark}[relation to the main theorem; observation of this note; \lab{proved}]\label{rem:L2vsmain}
A $\Lambda^2$-majorant $g^2$ with $g\in V_{\lambda/2}$ and $g\geq1$ on the
avoiders of a family is a nonnegative majorant of level $\lambda$, and it
is $\geq1$ on the avoiders that lie in $R_W$ (the setting of \cite[\S3]{TW2}
charges all primes).  So for families with
$M\leq P(M)^{1+B}$, $B$ fixed, Theorem~\ref{thm:karycap} applies to $g^2$
directly and gives $\log(1/\E g^2)\ll_B\lambda^{3/4}$ for $\lambda\geq\lambda_0(B)$,
with any number of large prime factors and no power of $\log L$.  Under
the $B$-hypothesis, Theorems~\ref{thm:twoprimecap} and~\ref{thm:rprime}
are therefore superseded as caps, as anticipated in \cite[\S11]{TW4}.  What the
$\Lambda^2$ route still gives beyond Theorem~\ref{thm:karycap} is the
removal of $B$ for a fixed number of large primes
(Theorem~\ref{thm:noB}), and the noise-stability tools themselves.
Without $B$, a $\Lambda^2$-majorant $g^2$ with $g\geq1$ on the \emph{whole}
avoider set of a family of the four types is a majorant in the sense of
Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}, which then gives $\log(1/\E g^2)\leq C\lambda^{3/4}$
for any number of large prime factors and with no $B$.  The settings of
\cite[\S3]{TW2} and \cite{TW4} are of this kind \cite[Lemma~7.1]{KA3}: the
admissible $g\in V_{\lambda/2}$ is required to be $\geq1$ on the avoiders of the
family $\cF$ itself (the fibre law and the base are devices of the proof,
not restrictions on $g$), the classes are $\cR(M)$-classes, and all primes
are charged, so $g^2$ is a CRT majorant of level $\leq\lambda$ in the sense of
Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}; the savings agree, both being
$\log(1/\E_Ug^2)$ under the uniform law.  Hence every $\Lambda^2$ cap of
this section (Theorems~\ref{thm:twoprimecap}, \ref{thm:rprime}
and~\ref{thm:noB}, and the uniformity statements after them) is superseded
as a cap by $\log(1/\E g^2)\leq CA_0^{3/4}L^{3/4}$ for every $r$, with or without
$B$, including the middle range $r\asymp\log L$ left open below
\lab{proved}.  \cite[Lemma~7.1]{KA3} was added after the review
\cite{KA3rev} and checked by the coordinator of this programme only.
What the $\Lambda^2$ route still gives is its mechanism (fibre tilting,
star sums, per-prime efficiency) and the noise-stability tools.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[what was bypassed]\label{rem:bypass}
The route of \cite{TW2} split the off-diagonal term into a same-label part
$(H_O^=)$ (proved there, \cite[Lemma~5.4]{TW2}) and a cross-label part
$(H_O^{\neq})$, both as \emph{un-quarantined} pair sums, with a sufficient
form $H_{\rm div}$ \cite[\S5.5]{TW2}.  Theorem~\ref{thm:HO} does not prove
$(H_O^{\neq})$ or $H_{\rm div}$: small partners are paid for through
$\min(x,1)^2\leq x$, which says nothing about pair sums.  Both remain
\lab{open} as stated, but are no longer needed.  The exponent budget
$\alpha^{-3}$ is used only by the small part, so this route does not
lower the exponent $3/4$.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Any fixed number of large primes, and removing \texorpdfstring{$B$}{B}}
The results of this subsection are from \cite{TW4}; the review
\cite{TW4rev} (two rounds) found them sound after nits and one minor
repair (G1), all applied.  Their new arithmetic input is an upper-bound
sieve for the \emph{whole} partner of a modulus, from the arithmetic large
sieve, in place of Brun--Titchmarsh for a prime partner.

\begin{lemma}[rough-partner Brun--Titchmarsh; {\cite[Lemma~2.3]{TW4}}; \lab{proved}]\label{lem:roughBT}
Let $s\geq1$, $w\geq2$, $q\geq1$, $(b,q)=1$ and $x\geq q\,2^{s+1}$.  Let
$\mathcal R_s(w)$ be the set of integers all of whose prime factors exceed
$w$ and with $\Omega(R)\leq s$.  Put $Y=x/q$, $Z=Y^{1/(s+1)}$ and
$H=\sum_{w<p\leq Z}1/(p-1)$ ($H=0$ if $Z\leq w$).  Then
\[
 \sum_{\substack{R\in\mathcal R_s(w),\ x<R\leq2x\\ R\equiv b\ (q)}}\frac1R
 \leq\frac{3(s+1)(1+H+\dots+H^{s-1})}{\varphi(q)\log Y}.
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Write $R=dn$, $d$ the part of $R$ at primes $\leq Z$.  If $d=R$ then
$R\leq Z^s<x$; so $\Omega(d)\leq s-1$, $d\leq Z^{s-1}$, $(d,q)=1$, and $n>1$
has all prime factors $>Z$, with $n\equiv b\bar d\pmod q$ and
$n\in(x/d,2x/d]$.  Fix $d$ and write $n=n_0+qk$.  The admissible $k$ lie in
an interval of at most $x/(dq)+1$ consecutive integers and avoid one class
modulo each prime $p\leq Z$, $p\nmid q$.  The arithmetic large sieve
\cite{MV} (in the form $|\mathcal N|\leq(N+Q^2)/J$) with $Q=Z$, and
$J\geq(\varphi(q)/q)\log Z$ by \cite{vLR}, give
$\#\{n\}\leq(x/(dq)+1+Z^2)/((\varphi(q)/q)\log Z)\leq3x/(d\varphi(q)\log Z)$,
since $x/(dq)\geq Z^2$ and $x/(dq)\geq1$.  Each $R$ has $1/R<1/x$.  Sum over
$d$: $\sum_d1/d\leq\sum_{i<s}H^i$, and $\log Z=\log Y/(s+1)$.
\end{proof}
For $s=1$ this is Brun--Titchmarsh up to the constant.  Neither the
primality of the partner nor its splitting is used.  In the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:rprime} below it takes the place of the
Brun--Titchmarsh step used in Theorem~\ref{thm:HO}, and it closes the ternary residual left open in \cite[\S6.3]{TW3}: there the
partner $R=\ell_a\ell_b$ with $\ell_b<w_2q$ is itself rough to level
$(R/q)/w_2$, so only an \emph{upper} bound for rough integers in one
progression of length $R/q$ is needed, not an asymptotic of
Bombieri--Friedlander--Iwaniec type \cite[\S2]{TW4}.

\begin{theorem}[$r$-prime $\Lambda^2$ cap; {\cite[Thm~7.1]{TW4}}; \lab{proved}]\label{thm:rprime}
Fix $r\geq2$, $B\geq0$ and $A_0\geq1$.  Let the family consist of classes
of $\cR(M)$ with $M\leq X$, $M\leq P(M)^{1+B}$, and at most $r$ distinct
prime factors of $M$ above $w_2=(\log X)^8$.  Then every $g\in V_{\lambda/2}$,
$\lambda\leq A_0\log X$, with $g\geq1$ on the avoiders of the family satisfies
\[
 \log\frac1{\E g^2}\ll_{A_0,B,r}L^{3/4}(\log L)^{3r+O(1)}.
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
The noise-stability input is the any-arity bound with codegree terms and
free codegree quarantine of \cite[Lemmas~6.1, 6.2]{TW3}; it reduces the
saving to star sums \cite[Prop.~3.1]{TW4}.  The fibre law of
\cite[\S3]{TW2} extends with threshold $\delta_r$ \cite[Lemma~4.1]{TW4}.
Whole-event and unary terms are bounded by Shiu along the top prime
($\ll\alpha^{-3}(\log L)^{r+O(1)}$), prime-power classes cost $o(1)$, small
partners are paid through $\min(x,1)^2\leq x$, and large partners through
a largest-variable reduction with Lemma~\ref{lem:roughBT} and box counting
modulo squarefree $Q$, $\ll(\log L)^{3r+O(1)}$
\cite[\S\S5--6]{TW4}.
\end{proof}

The $r$-dependence is explicit \cite[Prop.~9.1]{TW4} \lab{proved}: every
implied constant is $\leq(C_B\log L)^{Cr}$, so for $r\leq\epsilon\log L/\log\log L$,
$\epsilon\leq\epsilon_0$, the cap is $\ll_{A_0,B}L^{3/4+C\epsilon}$.  At the other end,
classes with at least $330\log L$ prime factors above $w_2$, with no
$B$-hypothesis, can be added to any such family at cost $o(1)$, provided
the fibre law also conditions on a low-mass event \cite[Lemma~9.2,
Cor.~9.3]{TW4} \lab{proved}.

\begin{theorem}[no $B$-hypothesis for fixed $r$; {\cite[Thm~10.4]{TW4}}; \lab{proved}]\label{thm:noB}
Fix $r\geq2$ and $A_0\geq1$.  For families of $\cR(M)$-classes with $M\leq X$
and at most $r$ distinct prime factors above $(\log X)^8$, with no
condition relating $M$ to $P(M)$, every $g\in V_{\lambda/2}$,
$\lambda\leq A_0\log X$, with $g\geq1$ on the avoiders satisfies
\[
 \log\frac1{\E g^2}\ll_{A_0,r}L^{3/4}(\log L)^{O_r(1)}.
\]
The same holds if the family also contains arbitrary $\cR(M)$-classes
with at least $330\log L$ prime factors above $(\log X)^8$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Write $M=kM_L$ with $k$ the $w_2$-smooth part.  The classes split into
\emph{smooth-dominated} ($k>M_L^2$), \emph{high-power} (not
smooth-dominated, $\ell^{12}\mid M$ for some $\ell>w_2$), and the rest,
which satisfy $M\leq P(M)^{33r}$ and are handled by
Theorem~\ref{thm:rprime} with $B=33r-1$.  For the smooth-dominated
classes, Cauchy--Schwarz, Rankin's trick on the smooth $k$ and Shiu's
theorem along $k$ (the modulus $M_L$ is now short) give a
polylogarithmic \emph{unweighted} first moment \cite[Lemmas~10.1,
10.2]{TW4}; the high-power classes have first moment $\ll L^{-3}$.  The
fibre law survives without $B$ \cite[Lemma~10.3]{TW4}.
\end{proof}
The middle range $\epsilon\log L/\log\log L<r<330\log L$ is not covered by the
$\Lambda^2$ method.  By a Poisson-type heuristic it carries most of the
$\alpha^{-3}$-weighted mass (\emph{Assessment}, \cite[\S9.3]{TW4}).  There the
method needs a hub count and an off-diagonal pair-sum bound that are
efficient per prime \cite[\S\S9.3, 12]{TW4} \lab{open}.  As a \emph{cap} the
middle range is no longer open: by Remark~\ref{rem:L2vsmain}, every
$\Lambda^2$ majorant of these settings saves at most $CA_0^{3/4}L^{3/4}$, for every
$r$ and without $B$ \cite[\S\S6--7]{KA3}.  The open question concerns only
the $\Lambda^2$-internal mechanism.

\section{Beyond CRT evaluation: rounding, primes and moments}\label{sec:noncrt}

The caps above concern bounds of the shape $N\E\nu+\sum_i|a_i|$ for
majorants $\geq0$ on $\Z$ and $\geq1$ on the whole avoider set.  This section
treats three natural ways out: signed rounding, majorants that are
positive only at primes, and moment methods.  Everything here is from
\cite{NC}, for the \emph{finite prime-slice} families of
Corollary~\ref{cor:sliceCap} only (not the sequential or balanced
settings of Sections~\ref{sec:gapped}--\ref{sec:main}); the review
\cite{NCrev} (two rounds) found it sound after repairs, which narrowed
the scope statements reproduced below, and led to the withdrawal of one
conjecture.

\subsection{Per-frequency rounding}
For a majorant $\nu$ with common period $Q_{\rm tot}$ put
$\hat\nu(\theta)=\E_n\nu(n)\e(-n\theta)$ for $\theta\in Q_{\rm tot}^{-1}\Z/\Z$, so
$\hat\nu(0)=\E\nu$ and, exactly,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:signed}
 \sum_{n\leq N}\nu(n)-N\E\nu=\sum_{\theta\neq0}\hat\nu(\theta)S_N(\theta),\qquad
 S_N(\theta)=\sum_{n\leq N}\e(n\theta).
\end{equation}
A \emph{per-frequency rounding bound} is
$R_w(\nu)=\sum_{\theta\neq0}|\hat\nu(\theta)|w(\theta)$ with a weight $w\geq1$.
Every class indicator has $\sum_\theta|\hat{\ind}(\theta)|=1$, so
$R_1(\nu)\leq\sum_i|a_i|$, and $R_1$ can be much smaller.  The weight
$w(\theta)=\min(N,1/(2\|\theta\|))$ makes $R_w$ a valid bound for
\eqref{eq:signed}, and complete Gauss, Kloosterman, Ramanujan or
divisor-exponential sums over the classes sharing one frequency are
already inside $|\hat\nu(\theta)|$.

For independent $x_i\sim\Bern(p_i)$ write $y_i=x_i-p_i$; every $f$ on
$\{0,1\}^I$ has a unique biased Walsh expansion $f=\sum_Sd_Sy^S$, $d_\emptyset=\E f$.

\begin{proposition}[Boolean sieve limit at arbitrary level; {\cite[Prop.~2.1]{NC}}; \lab{proved}]\label{prop:highlevel}
In the setting of Proposition~\ref{prop:boolean}, assume $p_i\leq1/4$ for
all $i$, and let $f\geq0$ with $f(0)\geq1$ be of \emph{any} level.  Put
$r_0=\sum_{s(S)>\lambda}|d_S|\prod_{i\in S}p_i$ and
$r_1=\sum_{s(S)>\lambda}|d_S|\prod_{i\in S}2p_i(1-p_i)$, $s(S)=\sum_{i\in S}s_i$.
Then $\E f\geq(1-r_0)\e^{-\Phi}-2r_1$, with $\Phi$ the right side of
\eqref{eq:boolean}.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Split $f=f_{\rm lo}+f_{\rm hi}$ at level $\lambda$.  Then $\E f=\E f_{\rm lo}$,
$f_{\rm lo}(0)\geq1-r_0$, $\E|f_{\rm hi}|\leq r_1$, and $f_{\rm lo}\geq-h$ with
$h=\E[|f_{\rm hi}|\mid x_{\{s_i\leq\lambda\}}]$.  Run the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:boolean} on $f_{\rm lo}$, carrying $h$: at the
interpolation step write $LQ\leq|L|(Q+H)+|L|H$ with $Q+H\geq0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Walsh coefficients versus Fourier mass; {\cite[Lemma~2.2]{NC}}; \lab{proved}]\label{lem:walshfourier}
Take a prime-slice system with $\cP$ finite and a majorant $\nu$ (any
moduli).  For $c\in R$ let $\nu_c=\sum_Sd_S(c)y^S$ be the conditional
expectation of $\nu$ given $n\equiv c\pmod{Q_0}$ and the hit indicators.  For
$S\neq\emptyset$ let $\Theta_S$ be the frequencies
$a/Q_0+\sum_{\ell\in S}h_\ell/\ell$ with every $h_\ell\not\equiv0\pmod\ell$, and
$A_S(\nu)=\sum_{\theta\in\Theta_S}|\hat\nu(\theta)|$.  Then
$|d_S(c)|\prod_{\ell\in S}p_\ell(c)(1-p_\ell(c))\leq A_S(\nu)\prod_{\ell\in S}p_\ell(c)$,
and $\sum_{S\neq\emptyset}A_S(\nu)\leq R_1(\nu)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
$d_S(c)\prod_Sp_\ell(1-p_\ell)=\E[\nu_cy^S]=Q_0\E_n[\nu(n)g(n)]$ with
$g=\ind[n\equiv c\ (Q_0)]\prod_{\ell\in S}y_\ell(n\bmod\ell)$.  By CRT, $\hat g$ is
supported on $\Theta_S$ with $|\hat g|\leq Q_0^{-1}\prod_Sp_\ell(c)$; apply
Parseval.  The sets $\Theta_S$ are pairwise disjoint and avoid $0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[sieve limit with per-frequency rounding; {\cite[Thm~2.3, Cors~2.4, 2.5]{NC}}; \lab{proved}; Case A \lab{proved mod} Elsholtz--Tao Prop.~1.4]\label{thm:perfreq}
Take a finite prime-slice system with $p_\ell(c)\leq1/4$, weights
$s_\ell=\log(1/(2p^+_\ell))$, $p^+_\ell=\max_cp_\ell(c)$, and a majorant $\nu$ of
arbitrary level.  Let $\bar\Phi(\lambda,\alpha)$ be the right side of
\eqref{eq:slice} minus $\log(Q_0/|R|)$, computed with these weights and
$s_*=\log2$.  Then for all $\lambda\geq s_*$ and $\alpha>0$,
\[
 \E\nu\geq\frac{|R|}{Q_0}\bigl[(1-\varepsilon)\e^{-\bar\Phi(\lambda,\alpha)}-2\varepsilon\bigr],\qquad
 \varepsilon=\sum_{s(S)>\lambda}A_S(\nu)\e^{-s(S)}\leq\e^{-\lambda}R_1(\nu).
\]
Consequently, for the families of Corollary~\ref{cor:sliceCap}, every
bound $\#(\A\cap[1,N])\leq N\E\nu+R_w(\nu)$ with $w\geq1$ has saving at most
$C(\log N)^{3/4}+\log(P/\varphi(P))$, with no bound on the size of the
slice primes.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
In each fibre apply Proposition~\ref{prop:highlevel}; by
Lemma~\ref{lem:walshfourier} both tails are at most $\varepsilon$.  Average
over $c$ as in Theorem~\ref{thm:slice}.  If the bound equals $N\e^{-s}$ then
$R_1\leq N$, and for $N\geq N_0(C)$ the level $\lambda=2\log(4N)$ gives $\varepsilon\leq\e^{-\bar\Phi}/4$.  For
the families of Corollary~\ref{cor:sliceCap}, $s_\ell\geq\frac{1-C}2\log\ell$
for $\ell\geq\ell_0(C)$, so the profile is $\ll\alpha^{-3}$ as there.
\end{proof}
Only the Fourier mass above level $\lambda$ enters.  This removes, for
finite prime-slice families, the proviso ``slice primes $\leq N^{O(1)}$''
(item~(A3) of Definition~\ref{def:arch}), and it covers the coefficient sum,
the sawtooth bound in the form $\min(N,1/(2\|\theta\|))$, and complete
exponential sums over the classes of one frequency.  It does \emph{not}
cover \cite[\S\S2.3--2.5]{NC}: Erd\H{o}s--Tur\'an/Vaaler rounding with the
factor $2|\sin\pi N\theta|$, per-class floor/ceiling rounding, the exact
weight $|S_N(\theta)|$, smooth windows, or Kloosterman/dispersion
cancellation over the moduli; these have weight $<1$ or cancel between
frequencies.  For smooth windows, $Q_0=1$ and hit-pattern majorants,
the cap follows from an equidistribution statement for sums
$\sum_{\ell\in S}h_\ell/\ell$ near $0$, Conjecture $(H_{\rm eq})$ of
\cite[(2.6)]{NC} \lab{conditional}; the other cases are \lab{open}.

\subsection{Prime-only majorants}
A \emph{prime majorant} is $\geq0$ at all primes and $\geq1$ at the primes of
$\A$; the bound is $\sum_{p\leq N}\nu(p)$.

\begin{theorem}[{\cite[Lemma~3.1, Thms~3.2, 3.3]{NC}}; \lab{proved}]\label{thm:primemaj}
(1) Assume $|F_\ell(c)\setminus\{0\}|\leq(\ell-1)/4$ (so in particular
$<\ell-1$).  By Dirichlet's theorem, a prime
majorant is $\geq0$ on every reduced class modulo a common period $L$ and
$\geq1$ on every reduced class contained in $\A$.  Theorem~\ref{thm:slice}
and Lemma~\ref{lem:budget} hold for the uniform law on $(\Z/L)^\times$, with
$\varphi(Q_0)$, $R\cap(\Z/Q_0)^\times$ and $p^*_\ell(c)=|F_\ell(c)\setminus\{0\}|/(\ell-1)$
in place of $Q_0,R,p_\ell(c)$ (the budget lemma up to a factor
$\ll\log\lambda$).  For the families of Corollary~\ref{cor:sliceCap} with a
selector, the cap $C(\log N)^{3/4}+\log(P/\varphi(P))$ at level $A\log N$ is
unchanged.
(2) If an integer majorant is $\geq1$ only on $\A\cap\{(n,P(z))=1\}$ with
$z\leq\e^\lambda$, has level $\leq\lambda$ in the system augmented by the class $0$
at each $\ell\leq z$ (the primes $\ell<5$ go into the selector), and the
augmented probabilities are $\leq1/4$, then the
bound of Theorem~\ref{thm:slice} grows by at most
$C_4\sum_{\ell\leq z}\ell^{-1-\alpha}+O(\log^2\lambda)$, i.e.\ by
$O((\log\log N)^2)$ at $\alpha=\lambda^{-1/4}$, $\lambda\asymp\log N$.
\end{theorem}
So prime equidistribution at level $N^{O(1)}$ (Siegel--Walfisz,
Bombieri--Vinogradov, Bombieri--Davenport--Halberstam,
Elliott--Halberstam, GRH) cannot lift a prime majorant above the cap.
That such majorants live at level $N^{O(1)}$, and that unconditional prime
error terms are weaker than the integer count at relative accuracy
$\e^{-(\log N)^{3/4}}$, is an \emph{Assessment} \cite[Remark~3.4]{NC}, not a
theorem.

\subsection{Moment methods}
For a finite witness family $\mathcal W$ of classes put
$f(n)=\#\{W\in\mathcal W:n\in W\}$.  If $P$ is a real polynomial with $P\geq0$
on $\Z_{\geq0}$ and $P(0)\geq1$, then $P\circ f$ is a CRT majorant of
$\{f=0\}$ \cite[Prop.~4.1]{NC} \lab{proved}.  Hence integer moment methods
of any order, whose moments are evaluated as $N\cdot$(CRT density) plus a
per-frequency error, are capped by Theorem~\ref{thm:perfreq}; prime moments
are capped at level $\leq A\log N$ by Theorem~\ref{thm:primemaj}.  Beyond
that level they are capped only under the Assessment of
\cite[Remark~3.4]{NC} (every high-level term costs $\geq|a_i|$) together
with $\sum_{\rm high}|a_i|<\pi(N)$ and slice primes $\leq N^{O(1)}$
\cite[Prop.~4.1]{NC}.  For slice witness classes
and $\deg P\leq k$, $\log(1/\E[P\circ f])\leq19k(2+\log^+(C_4\bar\mu/(19k)))+O(\log(\bar\mu+k))+\log(Q_0/|R|)$
\cite[Prop.~4.2]{NC} \lab{proved}; with witness moduli $\leq N^{O(1)}$,
$\bar\mu\ll(\log N)^3$ and a degree-$k$
method saves $O(k\log\log N)$ in the CRT mean, so the second moment gives
only a power of $\log N$.

\subsection{Direct interval counts}
What is left of signed rounding is cancellation \emph{between}
frequencies in \eqref{eq:signed}, i.e.\ a direct count of
$\sum_{n\leq N}\nu(n)$.  Theorem~\ref{thm:perfreq} turns into a dichotomy
\cite[Thm~8.1]{NC} \lab{proved}: if $\sum_{n\leq N}\nu(n)\leq N\e^{-s}$ then, for
every $\lambda$ and $\alpha$, either the Fourier mass above level $\lambda$ satisfies
$\varepsilon>\e^{-\bar\Phi(\lambda,\alpha)}/4$, or
$N\E\nu-\sum_{n\leq N}\nu(n)\geq N[(|R|/(4Q_0))\e^{-\bar\Phi}-\e^{-s}]$.  For the
families of Corollary~\ref{cor:sliceCap}, $\bar\Phi(\lambda)\leq C\lambda^{3/4}$, so for
$s=(\log N)^\theta$, $\theta>3/4$, the second branch is non-vacuous at every level
$\lambda\leq c(\log N)^{4\theta/3}$ (a range containing levels $\gg\log N$): there
a direct count needs either Fourier mass $\gtrsim\e^{-C\lambda^{3/4}}$ above level
$\lambda$, or an interval count a factor $\geq\e^{s-C\lambda^{3/4}}$ below the CRT
mean \cite[\S8.1]{NC}.  Two facts about $[1,N]$ are proved.  Case-B classes of modulus
$>M_0:=8\lfloor(N+1)/3\rfloor^2$ contain no $n\leq N$, so for hit-pattern
majorants they give neither sieving power nor cost \cite[Lemma~8.2,
Cor.~8.3]{NC}; and the multiplier-one witnesses of all moduli have mean
$\leq\frac14(1+1/N)(1+\log N)^2$ on $[1,N]$ \cite[Prop.~8.4]{NC}.  The moduli in
$(N,M_0]$ still carry real hit mass $\asymp(\log N)^3$ per integer, a
constant factor below the CRT value \lab{evidence}; a conjecture of
\cite{NC} to the contrary was withdrawn after review.  For hit-count
polynomials of degree $k$ evaluated exactly on $[1,N]$, beating $3/4$ needs
$k\geq(\log N)^{3/4+o(1)}$, \lab{conditional} on a local-limit hypothesis
$H_{\rm node}$ for the witness count, which is untested, and under the
further hypothesis that the mean witness count on $[1,N]$ is
$m\geq16(\log N)^{3/4+\delta}$ \cite[Prop.~8.6, Cor.~8.7]{NC}.  For the full
Case-B family this hypothesis is supported only by enumeration and a
heuristic ($m\asymp(\log N)^3$, \lab{evidence}).  Numerically, for polynomials of degree $\leq10$ in the hit
count of a prime-modulus subfamily, the exact interval optimum saves less
than the CRT optimum \lab{evidence} \cite[\S8.3]{NC}.

\section{Large sieves, prime laws and interval counts}\label{sec:LS}

This section summarises four further internal documents: \cite{LS}
(review \cite{LSrev}), \cite{LS2} (reviews \cite{LS2rev} of \S\S1--7 and
\cite{LS2revb} of \S\S8--9), \cite{PL} (review \cite{PLrev}) and \cite{IF}
(review \cite{IFrev}).  No review found a fatal or major defect; the minor
repairs are applied in the sources.  Throughout, $\cG$ is a finite mixture
of the four types of Definition~\ref{def:types} with arbitrary moduli,
$\A=\A(\cG)\subseteq\Z$, and $M_0$ is the lcm of its moduli.  The \emph{level}
of a modulus $d$, or of a rational frequency of denominator $d$, is
$\lambda(d)=\sum_{\ell\mid d,\,\ell>W}\log\ell$, with $W$ from
Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}.  For a common period $M'$ fixed in each statement,
$\mathcal M(\A)$ is the set of probability measures on $\Z/M'$ supported on
$\A\bmod M'$, and $\hat\rho(\theta)=\sum_n\rho(n)\e(n\theta)$.

The sources cite \cite[Thm~5.1]{KA2}, with cap $C\lambda^{3/4}(\log\lambda)^{3/4}$.  The
integer-measure results use it only as a black-box lower bound for $\E\nu$,
so Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap} may be substituted, giving $C\lambda^{3/4}$ and the
reading $C_A(\log N)^{3/4}$ (\cite[\S4.3]{KA3}, pointer level; below it is a
one-line step in each proof).  The unit-measure results
(Theorem~\ref{thm:PLcap} and its consequences) rerun the proof instead; for
them the improvement is pointer-level only, and both forms are stated.  Case-A classes enter only through the cap
used (Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}, Theorem~\ref{thm:PLcap} or
Corollary~\ref{cor:sliceCap}) and carry its dependence on
\cite{ElsholtzTao}; Theorem~\ref{thm:gallagher} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:compkernel}(3) have none.

\subsection{Duality and the cap}
A finite $\Theta\subset\mathbb R/\Z$ with weights $w_\theta>0$ is an
\emph{$N$-large-sieve system} if
$\sum_\theta w_\theta|\sum_{n\in I}a_n\e(n\theta)|^2\leq\sum_{n\in I}|a_n|^2$ for every
interval $I$ of $N$ consecutive integers: Montgomery--Vaughan \cite{MV}, the
Farey form \cite{Montgomery} with any prime, prime-power or composite
moduli, weighted forms.  Always $w_\theta\leq1/N$ (take $a_n=\e(-n\theta)$) and
$\sum_\theta w_\theta\leq1$ (random signs).  Let $A\subseteq\A\cap I$, $Z=|A|$, $\Theta_{\mathbb Q}$ the
rational points of $\Theta$, $M'$ a common multiple of $M_0$ and their
denominators, and $F_w(\rho)=\sum_{\theta\in\Theta_{\mathbb Q}}w_\theta|\hat\rho(\theta)|^2$.
With $a_n=\ind_A$ the inequality gives $Z^2F_w(\rho_A)\leq Z$, $\rho_A$ the law of
$A\bmod M'$.  A bound $Z\leq1/L$ is \emph{CRT-admissible} if $L\leq F_w(\rho)$
for every $\rho\in\mathcal M(\A)$, as in every arithmetic large sieve in use.
Irrational frequencies are excluded by definition.

\begin{theorem}[duality; {\cite[Thm~2.1, Cor.~2.2]{LS}}; standard; \lab{proved}]\label{thm:LSdual}
Put $F^*_w=\min_{\rho\in\mathcal M(\A)}F_w(\rho)$ and
$m_w=\inf\{\sum_\theta|\gamma_\theta|^2/w_\theta:\ g=\sum_{\theta\in\Theta_{\mathbb Q}}\gamma_\theta\e(-n\theta),\ \mathrm{Re}\,g\geq1\text{ on }\A\}$.
Then $F^*_w=1/m_w$, attained when finite.  For an $N$-large-sieve system
every CRT-admissible bound has $1/L\geq m_w\geq N\cdot\E\nu^*$, where
$\nu^*=|g^*|^2$ for an optimal $g^*$.  $\nu^*$ is $\geq0$ on $\Z$, $\geq1$ on $\A$,
and a real combination of classes with moduli dividing
$\lcm(\mathrm{den}\,\theta,\mathrm{den}\,\theta')$, $\theta,\theta'\in\Theta_{\mathbb Q}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
For feasible $g$,
$1\leq\mathrm{Re}\sum_n\rho(n)g(n)=\mathrm{Re}\sum_\theta\gamma_\theta\overline{\hat\rho(\theta)}$,
and Cauchy--Schwarz gives $F_w(\rho)\geq1/m_w$.  The reverse is the minimax
theorem on $\mathcal M(\A)$ times the unit ball.  Finally
$\sum|\gamma|^2/w\geq N\sum|\gamma|^2=N\E|g^*|^2$ by $w\leq1/N$ and Parseval.
\end{proof}
This is the classical large-sieve/Selberg duality, not claimed as new.
There is no rounding term, since the factor $N$ sits in the large-sieve
constant; only the \emph{level} of $\nu^*$ matters.  The multiplicative
sieve is covered: by Gauss sums,
$q\sum_{\chi\text{ prim}}|\sum_n\rho(n)\chi(n)|^2\leq\varphi(q)\sum^*_{a\bmod q}|\hat\rho(a/q)|^2$,
so the character functional lies pointwise below the Farey one
\cite[Rem.~2.5]{LS}.  For prime product systems Montgomery's sieve is
CRT-optimal, $F^*_1=S(Q)$ \cite[Prop.~2.6]{LS} \lab{proved}.

\emph{Fibrewise sieves.}  Fix $Q_0\leq N/2$.  In the fibre $c\bmod Q_0$ write
$n=c+Q_0m$, with $m$ in an interval of $N_c\geq\lfloor N/Q_0\rfloor$ integers,
avoiding $\A_c=\{m:c+Q_0m\in\A\}$.  Each fibre uses its own
$N_c$-large-sieve system with a CRT-admissible $L_c$, or the trivial bound;
$B=\sum_c1/L_c$.  Let $\lambda_\Theta$ be the largest level of a rational
frequency used.

\begin{theorem}[large-sieve cap; {\cite[Thm~3.1, Cor.~3.2]{LS}}, {\cite[\S4.3]{KA3}}; \lab{proved} via Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}; Case A as there]\label{thm:LScap}
With $\lambda=\max(\lambda_0,\lambda(Q_0)+2\lambda_\Theta)$, every fibrewise CRT-admissible
large-sieve bound for $A\subseteq\A\cap I$, $|I|=N$, has
$\log(N/B)\leq\log2+C\lambda^{3/4}$.  If $Q_0\leq\min(N^A,N/2)$ and every
frequency has denominator $\leq N^A$, the saving is $\leq C_A(\log N)^{3/4}$.
So no CRT-admissible large sieve with polynomially bounded denominators
(any moduli, forced classes used in any form, any weights, fibrewise or
not) proves $E(N)\ll N\exp\{-(\log N)^\theta\}$ with $\theta>3/4$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
In fibre $c$, Theorem~\ref{thm:LSdual} for the $M_0$-periodic set $\A_c$ gives
$1/L_c\geq N_c\E\nu_c$; put $\nu_c\equiv1$ in trivial fibres, $\nu_c\equiv0$ if
$\A_c=\emptyset$.  Let $\nu(n)=\nu_c((n-c)/Q_0)$ for $n\equiv c\pmod{Q_0}$.  A term
$a\ind[m\equiv b\pmod d]$ becomes $a\ind[n\equiv c+Q_0b\pmod{Q_0d}]$, so $\nu$ is a
majorant of $\A$ of level $\leq\lambda$, and
$B\geq\lfloor N/Q_0\rfloor Q_0\E\nu\geq(N/2)\E\nu$.  Apply Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}, which
charges only primes $>W$ (so smooth parts such as Farey $q=2^k$ are free).
For the reading, $\lambda\leq3A\log N$.
\end{proof}
Averaging $g^*$ modulo $M_0$ kills every frequency whose denominator does
not divide $M_0$, so $\lambda_\Theta$ may be computed on those only
\cite[Rem.~3.3]{LS}.  The large sieve of the $2/3$ note \cite{LL} is an
instance ($Q_0\leq N^{2\delta}$, prime moduli, $Q=(N/Q_0)^{1/2}$, atoms $\cR(k\ell)$
with $k\ell\leq\ell^{1+o(1)}$), capped with Theorem~\ref{thm:Bcapall} in place of
Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}.  So are \cite{Vaughan} and \cite[\S4]{PW}, on the
$2/3$ note's description of them; neither was re-read \cite[Rem.~3.4]{LS}.
This supersedes Remark~\ref{rem:LS}.

\begin{theorem}[prime slices, any rational frequencies; {\cite[Thm~4.1, Cor.~4.2, Rem.~4.4]{LS}}; \lab{proved}; Case A \lab{proved mod} Elsholtz--Tao Prop.~1.4]\label{thm:LSslice}
Take a prime-slice system (Definition~\ref{def:slice}) with
$f_\ell(c)=|F_\ell(c)|\leq\min(\ell/2,\ell^\kappa)$, $0\leq\kappa<1$, and
$\bar p_\ell=\E_{c\in R}f_\ell(c)/\ell$.  Every CRT-admissible bound $B$ from any
$N$-large-sieve system, with any rational frequencies, satisfies for
$0<\alpha\leq1-\kappa$
\[
 \log(N/B)\leq\frac{\alpha\log N}{2(1-\kappa)}+\log\frac{2Q_0}{|R|}+8\sum_\ell\bar p_\ell\ell^{-\alpha}.
\]
For the families of Corollary~\ref{cor:sliceCap} with all slice primes
$\geq\ell_0'(C)$ (an enlarged threshold) the saving is
$\leq C'(C)(\log N)^{3/4}+\log(P/\varphi(P))+\log2$, also fibrewise with
$Q_0\leq N/2$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Let $\beta=\alpha/(2(1-\kappa))$, $p'=2+2\beta$, and let $\rho$ be the product measure (residues
mod $\ell$ uniform outside $F_\ell(c)$) on the at least $|R|/2$ fibres of at most
twice the mean supply.  Hausdorff--Young on $\Z/Q_0$ gives
$\sum_\theta|\hat\rho(\theta)|^{p'}\leq(2Q_0/|R|)\exp\{8\sum\bar p_\ell\ell^{-\alpha}\}$; H\"older with
$\sum w\leq1$, $w\leq1/N$ bounds $F_w(\rho)$.  Then take $\alpha=(\log N)^{-1/4}$.
\end{proof}
For composite moduli, the Euler product of the prime-local system obtained
by conditioning on small primes does \emph{not} dominate the large sieve
\cite[Ex.~5.2]{LS} \lab{proved}; the top-prime sequential functional does,
through Theorem~\ref{thm:LScap}.

\subsection{Twisted, hybrid and prime large sieves}
\begin{lemma}[comparison measure; {\cite[Lemmas~1.1, 1.2]{LS2}}; \lab{proved} via Theorems~\ref{thm:noBcap}, \ref{thm:PLcap}; Case A as there]\label{lem:compmeas}
Let $\mathcal D\ni1$ be a finite set of moduli of level $\leq\lambda$, $\lambda\geq\lambda_0$,
$M'$ a common multiple of $M_0$ and $\mathcal D$, $V_{\mathcal D}$ the real span of
the classes with moduli in $\mathcal D$, and $\E_U$ the uniform mean on $\Z/M'$.
Some $\rho\in\mathcal M(\A)$ has $\E_\rho f\leq\e^{C\lambda^{3/4}}\E_Uf$ for every
$f\in V_{\mathcal D}$ with $f\geq0$.  The same holds on units: $\rho$ on
$\A\cap(\Z/M')^\times$, $\E^*$ uniform on units, $f\geq0$ on units, and
$\e^{S^*(\lambda)}$ with $S^*$ from Theorem~\ref{thm:PLcap}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The linear programme $m^*=\min\{\E_U\nu:\nu\in V_{\mathcal D},\ \nu\geq\ind_\A\}$ has
$m^*\geq\e^{-C\lambda^{3/4}}$ by Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}.  Duality gives $\mu\geq0$ with
$\sum_x\mu(x)\nu(x)=\E_U\nu$ on $V_{\mathcal D}$ and $\mu(\A)=m^*$; take $\rho=\mu|_\A/m^*$.
\end{proof}
One measure tests every nonnegative low-level function, not only
majorants, so Cauchy--Schwarz can strip twists and fibres.

A \emph{periodic Bessel system} of length $N$ consists of periodic rows
$\phi_j$ (period $d_j$) and $\Delta>0$ with
$\sum_j|\sum_{n\in I}a_n\overline{\phi_j(n)}|^2\leq\Delta\sum_{n\in I}|a_n|^2$ for every
interval $I$ of length $N$; its level is $\max_j\lambda(d_j)$.  Examples:
$\sqrt{w_\theta}\,\e(n\theta)$ ($\Delta=1$); $(q/\varphi(q))^{1/2}\chi$, $\chi$ primitive mod
$q\leq Q$ ($\Delta=N+Q^2$); hybrid rows $\chi(n)\e(n\theta)$; Gauss-sum rows.  It is
applied to $a_n=\psi(n)\ind_A(n)$ with a periodic twist, $|\psi|\geq1$ on $\A$; a
bound $Z\leq\Delta/L$ is CRT-admissible if
$L\leq\sum_j|\E_\rho[\psi\bar\phi_j]|^2/\E_\rho|\psi|^2$ for all $\rho\in\mathcal M(\A)$.  A
\emph{fibrewise hybrid} over $Q_0\leq N/2$ uses in each fibre (i) such a
system, (ii) a majorant $\nu_c$ of $\A_c$ with bound $N_c\E\nu_c+\sum|a_i|$, or
(iii) the trivial bound $N_c$; $B=\sum_cB_c$.
