\begin{theorem}[hybrid cap; {\cite[Thm~2.4, Cor.~2.5]{LS2}}; \lab{proved} via Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}; Case A as there]\label{thm:hybrid}
Let the type-(i) systems have level $\leq\lambda_\Phi$, every prime of $\cG$ be
$\leq\e^{\Lambda_0}$, and $\lambda=\max(\lambda_0,\lambda(Q_0)+\max(2\lambda_\Phi,\Lambda_0+\log N))$.  Then
$B\geq(N/2)\e^{-C\lambda^{3/4}}$.  If $Q_0\leq\min(N^A,N/2)$ and all row periods and
all primes of $\cG$ are $\leq N^A$, the saving is $\leq C_A(\log N)^{3/4}$.  The
twist's period is unrestricted.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Averaging the adjoint inequality over shifts of $I$ shows that
every $c$ has $N\E_U|\sum_jc_j\phi_j|^2\leq\Delta\|c\|^2$.  Take $\rho$ from Lemma~\ref{lem:compmeas}
with $\mathcal D$ containing $1$, $Q_0$, $Q_0\lcm(d_j,d_k)$ and the coarsened
fibre moduli, and its fibre laws $\rho_c$.  Type~(i): Cauchy--Schwarz
removes $\psi$, and the lifted $|\sum_jc_j\bar\phi_j|^2\geq0$ lies in $V_{\mathcal D}$, so
$B_c\geq N_cQ_0\rho(c)\e^{-S}$.  Type~(ii): Lemma~\ref{lem:budget} coarsens $\nu_c$ to
level $\leq\Lambda_0+\log N_c$ and mean $\leq B_c/N_c$; then
$1\leq\E_{\rho_c}\nu'_c\leq\e^S\E\nu'_c/(Q_0\rho(c))$.  Type~(iii): $\rho(c)\leq\e^S/Q_0$.
Sum over $c$.
\end{proof}
So twisted, multiplicative, Gauss-sum and hybrid large sieves, and
fibrewise mixtures with rounding fibres, give no $\theta>3/4$.  Not covered:
non-periodic rows ($n^{it}$, smooth weights in $n$), which use the position
of $n$ in $I$.

\begin{theorem}[the large sieve for primes; {\cite[Thm~3.1]{LS2}}; \lab{proved} via Theorem~\ref{thm:PLcap}]\label{thm:LSprimes}
Let $A$ consist of primes $p\leq N$, $p\in\A$, $p\nmid M'$; let admissibility
range over all probabilities on $\A\cap(\Z/M')^\times$, and let all fibres be of
type (i) or (iii), $c\in(\Z/Q_0)^\times$.  Then
$B\geq(N/2)\e^{-S^*(\lambda)}\varphi(M')/M'$, $\lambda=\max(\lambda_0,\lambda(Q_0)+2\lambda_\Phi)$.  If all
primes of $M'$ (family, rows, twists, $Q_0$) are $\leq N^A$ and $\lambda\leq3A\log N$,
the saving against $\pi(N)$ is $\leq C_A(\log N)^{3/4}(\log\log N)^{3/4}$, and
$\leq C_A(\log N)^{3/4}$ at pointer level.
\end{theorem}
The proof is that of Theorem~\ref{thm:hybrid} with the unit measure, using
$\E^*f\leq(M'/\varphi(M'))\E_Uf$ for $f\geq0$ and $M'/\varphi(M')\leq2A\log N$.

\subsection{The larger sieve}
For moduli $q\in\mathcal S$ with weights $w(q)\geq0$ let $K(m)=\sum_qw(q)\ind[q\mid m]$,
$W_K=K(0)$, $h\geq\max_{0<|m|<N}K(m)$, $\mathrm{coll}_q(\rho)=\sum_b\rho(b\bmod q)^2$ and
$D(\rho)=\sum_qw(q)\mathrm{coll}_q(\rho)$.  Counting pairs gives
$Z^2D(\rho_A)\leq ZW_K+(Z^2-Z)h$, so $Z\leq(W_K-h)/(L-h)$ if $h<L\leq D(\rho_A)$; the
bound is CRT-admissible if $L\leq D(\rho)$ for all $\rho\in\mathcal M(\A)$.
Gallagher's larger sieve \cite{Gallagher} takes $w=\Lambda$ on prime powers
$\leq Q$, $h=\log N$ and $L=\sum\Lambda(q)/\nu(q)$, $\nu(q)\geq|\A\bmod q|$.  With
$\chi^2_q(\rho)=q\,\mathrm{coll}_q(\rho)-1$ and $X(\rho)=\sum_q(w(q)/q)\chi^2_q(\rho)$, every
CRT-admissible kernel bound satisfies
$\log(N/B)\leq\log(1+NX(\rho)/(W_K-h))$ for every $\rho\in\mathcal M(\A)$
\cite[Thm~6.2]{LS} \lab{proved}, since $\sum_qw(q)/q\leq h+(W_K-h)/N$.  On
the prime slices of Corollary~\ref{cor:sliceCap} Gallagher's sieve saves
$O(1)$ \cite[Cor.~6.3]{LS}.

\begin{theorem}[Gallagher's sieve over any mixture; {\cite[Thm~4.3]{LS2}}; \lab{proved}, unconditional]\label{thm:gallagher}
There are absolute $C,N_0$ such that for every $\cG$ (Case A included) and
$Q\geq2$ some $\rho\in\mathcal M(\A)$ has
$\sum_{q=\ell^v\leq Q}(\Lambda(q)/q)\chi^2_q(\rho)\leq24\log\log(3Q)+C$.  Hence for $N\geq N_0$
Gallagher's larger sieve, in CRT-optimal or Cauchy--Schwarz form, with any
$Q$, saves at most $26\log\log N+C$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Take $W=\max(W_0,(\log3Q)^8)$, the sequential law $\sigma$ of
Section~\ref{sec:kary} on the base $R_W^\square$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:squarebase}),
singletons at the primes $\ell>W$, and $\rho=\sigma(\cdot\mid\A)$.  The leak is
$\leq C(\log W)^cW^{-1/4}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:secondnoB}, uniformly in $W$).  At $\ell>W$,
$\chi^2_{\ell^v}(\sigma)\ll(\log W)^c\ell^{-7/8+o(1)}$; at $p\leq W$, $\chi^2\leq7$, and Mertens gives
$3\log W+C$.
\end{proof}
It uses Shiu's and Mertens' theorems only, not \cite{ElsholtzTao} or
Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}.

\begin{theorem}[composite kernels; {\cite[Thms~4.2, 9.1, Prop.~9.2]{LS2}}; \lab{proved} via Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}, except (3)]\label{thm:compkernel}
(1) If every kernel modulus has level $\leq\lambda_{\mathcal S}$ and
$\lambda=\max(\lambda_0,2\lambda_{\mathcal S})$, every CRT-admissible kernel bound has
$B\geq(N/2)\e^{-C\lambda^{3/4}}/(1+Nh/(W_K-h))$.
(2) The same holds with no level hypothesis and $\lambda=\max(\lambda_0,2\log N+\Lambda_0)$
if all primes of $\cG$ and of the kernel moduli are $\leq\e^{\Lambda_0}$.
(3) \lab{proved}, unconditional: if $T\geq W_0$ and every kernel modulus has
no prime factor $\leq T$ and at most $T^{1/4}$ prime factors, the saving is
$\leq\log(1+\varepsilon_T+\varepsilon_TNh/(W_K-h))$, $\varepsilon_T=C(\log T)^cT^{-1/4}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
$D(\rho)=\sum_\theta\tilde w_\theta|\hat\rho(\theta)|^2$ with $0\leq\tilde w_\theta\leq h+(W_K-h)/N$; apply
Lemma~\ref{lem:compmeas} as in Theorem~\ref{thm:hybrid}.  (2) Cut $q\geq N$ to a
prefix $q^\circ\geq N$ of level $\leq\log N+\Lambda_0$: $\mathrm{coll}_q(\rho)\leq\e^S/N$.  (3) The
law of Theorem~\ref{thm:gallagher} with $W=T$.
\end{proof}
Open: kernels with $Nh/(W_K-h)\geq\exp\{(\log N)^{3/4}\}$ whose moduli have a
prime factor $\leq(Nh/(W_K-h))^{4+o(1)}$ or more than $T^{1/4}$ prime factors
\lab{open}.  No failing example is known.

\subsection{What remains of the large sieve}
Of the escapes of \cite[\S7]{LS} there remain: (E1) frequencies of
super-polynomial level against classes with several large primes; (E2)
composite kernels as above; (E3) non-CRT information; (E4) the exclusions
inherited from Theorem~\ref{thm:main}.

\begin{proposition}[what (E1) needs; {\cite[Props~5.1, 5.2]{LS2}}; \lab{proved}; (1) via Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}]\label{prop:E1}
(1) Let all primes of $\cG$ be $\leq N^A$.  If every frequency has at most $r$
distinct primes $>W$ in its denominator, the saving is
$\leq\log2+C(2rA\log N+\lambda(Q_0))^{3/4}$.  So a saving $\geq(\log N)^{3/4+\varepsilon}$
needs denominators with $\geq(\log N)^{4\varepsilon/3-o(1)}$ distinct family primes.
(2) If some $\rho\in\mathcal M(\A)$ has $\E_\rho f\leq\e^{S_1}\E_Uf$ for all $f\geq0$ of level
$\leq2\lambda'$, and $|\hat\rho(\theta)|^2\leq\e^{S_2}/N$ for all $\theta$ of level $>\lambda'$, then
every CRT-admissible large-sieve bound, with any rational frequencies and
weights, is $\geq N/(\e^{S_1}+\e^{S_2})$.
\end{proposition}
Part (2) has no cross terms, since $F_w$ is diagonal in $\theta$.

\begin{conjecture}[$(H_{{\rm LS}\infty})$; {\cite[\S5]{LS2}}; \lab{conjecture}]\label{conj:HLS}
For every family $\cG$ of the four types with primes $\leq N^{O(1)}$ there are
a fixed $\eta>0$ and $\rho\in\mathcal M(\A)$ satisfying Lemma~\ref{lem:compmeas} at
level $2\lambda'=(2/\eta)\log N$ and $|\hat\rho(\theta)|\leq\e^{C\lambda'^{3/4}}\e^{-\eta\lambda(\theta)}$ for
every $\theta$ of level $>\lambda'$.
\end{conjecture}
By Proposition~\ref{prop:E1}(2) this caps every CRT-admissible large sieve
at $C_\eta(\log N)^{3/4}$, whatever the frequencies.  The sequential law decays
only at the top prime of the denominator; a cluster expansion suggests
$\eta=1/2-o(1)$ (\emph{Assessment}).  A proof must use the sparsity of forced
classes:

\begin{theorem}[dense band families escape; {\cite[Lemma~8.1, Props~8.2, 8.4, 8.6, Thm~8.5]{LS2}}, {\cite{LS2revb}}; \lab{proved}]\label{thm:band}
Let $0<\eta\leq1/8$, let $\ell_i,\ell'_i\geq5$ ($i\leq K$) be distinct primes,
$D_i=\ell_i\ell'_i$, $\gcd(a_i,D_i)=1$, and $\A=\bigcap_i\{n:\|na_i/D_i\|\leq1/2-\eta\}$: an
abstract class family with $\asymp\eta D_i$ classes mod $D_i$.
(1) If all $\ell_i,\ell'_i>W$ and $\log D_i>\lambda$ for all $i$, every majorant of
level $\leq\lambda$ has $\E\nu\geq1$.
(2) Let $\eta=1/9$, $R=324$, $K=\lfloor\log(N/3)/\log(2R+1)\rfloor$ and $D_i\geq16KRN$.
For suitable $a_i$, a Montgomery--Vaughan large sieve with $\leq N/3$
frequencies, each nonzero one with denominator a product of some $D_i$,
gives $|\A\cap I|\leq(5N/3)\e^{-K/27}$ for every interval $I$ of length $N$: a
saving $\geq c\log N$, $c=1/(27\log649)-o(1)$.
(3) If moreover all $\ell_i,\ell'_i\in[N^{1/2+\delta},2N^{1/2+\delta}]$ with fixed small
$\delta>0$, then for $N\geq N_0$ every majorant of every level $\lambda'\geq\lambda_0$ has
$\log(1/\E\nu)\leq C\lambda'^{3/4}$, and (2) still holds.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
(1) Gale's supply--demand theorem gives a measure on the allowed classes
mod $D_i$ with uniform marginals mod $\ell_i$ and $\ell'_i$; the product measure
lives on $\A$ and agrees with $\E_U$ on classes of level $<\log D_i$.  (2) A
Fej\'er construction gives $P$ of degree $R$, $P\geq1$ on $\|t\|\leq1/2-\eta$,
$\int P^2\leq1-\eta/3$; with $a_i/D_i$ near $(2R+1)^{-i}$, Cauchy--Schwarz and \cite{MV}
applied to $g=\prod_iP(na_i/D_i)\geq1$ on $\A$ give $|A|\leq(5N/3)(1-\eta/3)^K$.  (3) Use
(1) below level $\min_i\log D_i$; above it Theorem~\ref{thm:kcomp} gives
$O((\lambda'/\log N+1)\log\log N)$, and the density of $\A$ gives $0.04\log N$.
\end{proof}
So (E1) cannot be closed by comparison measures at any or every level plus
the large-sieve axioms.  The band family puts mass $\asymp\eta$ on single
moduli of high level.  Forced families are expected to satisfy (Sp): for
$D$ of level $\geq\log N$, the classes with modulus divisible by $D$ carry
mass $\leq D^{-1+o(1)}$ (\emph{Assessment}, not proved).  A proof of
Conjecture~\ref{conj:HLS} must use (Sp) or the moment hypotheses of
Section~\ref{sec:noB}.  No method in the literature uses such frequencies.

\subsection{Prime-only majorants for mixtures}
A \emph{prime majorant} of $\A$ is $\nu=\sum_ia_i\ind[n\equiv b_i\pmod{d_i}]$ with
$\nu(p)\geq0$ at all primes and $\nu(p)\geq1$ at all primes $p\in\A$, outside a
finite set of primes.  Let $L$ be the lcm of the $d_i$ and the moduli of
$\cG$, and $\E^*$ the uniform mean on $(\Z/L)^\times$.  By Dirichlet's theorem,
$\nu$ is a prime majorant iff $\nu\geq0$ on every reduced class mod $L$ and
$\nu\geq1$ on every reduced class contained in $\A$.  Under $\E^*$ the
prime-power coordinates are independent and uniform on units, and
selector classes have mass $0$ \cite[Lemmas~1.2, 1.3]{PL}.

\begin{theorem}[prime-majorant cap; {\cite[Thm~3.1]{PL}}; \lab{proved}; Case A \lab{proved mod} Elsholtz--Tao Prop.~1.4]\label{thm:PLcap}
There are absolute $W,\lambda_0,C$ such that every prime majorant $\nu$ of level
$\lambda\geq\lambda_0$ of $\A(\cG)$, $\cG$ any finite mixture of the four types with
arbitrary moduli, has $\log(1/\E^*\nu)\leq S^*(\lambda):=C\lambda^{3/4}(\log\lambda)^{3/4}$, and
$\leq C(B)\lambda^{3/4}$ if every modulus satisfies $G\leq P(G)^{1+B}$.  By
\cite[\S4.3]{KA3}, $S^*(\lambda)=C\lambda^{3/4}$ with Case A as in
Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}; this is pointer-level and not separately reviewed.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Run the proof of \cite[Thm~5.1]{KA2} on the unit groups $(\Z/\ell^e)^\times$.  The
square base consists of units, since no forced class contains a unit
square (Lemma~\ref{lem:nosquare}); its $R$-term is $(\pi(W)+1)\log2$.  The
sequential steps are arithmetic-free, non-unit classes have mass $0$, and
the factor $\ell/(\ell-1)$ on unit classes is absorbed into the inflation weight
$\gamma^*(\ell)=(\ell/(\ell-1))(1-\ell^{-1/2})^{-1}$ of the moments and the leak
\cite[Lemmas~2.3--2.5]{PL}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[prime-law methods; {\cite[Cor.~4.2]{PL}}; \lab{proved}; Case A as in Theorem~\ref{thm:PLcap}]\label{cor:PLmethods}
Let a method bound $\#\{p\leq N:p\in\A(\cG)\}$ by $\pi(N)\E^*\nu+\mathrm{Err}$, with $\nu$ a
prime majorant and $\mathrm{Err}\geq0$ (including the correction
$\sum_{p\mid L,\,p\leq N}(1+|\nu(p)|)$).  Assume either that for every modulus of
$\nu$ the product of its distinct primes $>W$ is $\leq N^A$, or that every
prime of $\cG$ is $\leq N^A$ and $\sum_i|a_i|\leq N^A$.  Then the saving against
$\pi(N)$ is $\leq C_A(\log N)^{3/4}(\log\log N)^{3/4}$; it is $\leq C_{A,B}(\log N)^{3/4}$
under bounded $B$, and $\leq C_A(\log N)^{3/4}$ in general at pointer level.
\end{corollary}
This is the form of every method that evaluates $\sum_{p\leq N}\nu(p)$ by
Dirichlet main terms with errors bounded in absolute value:
Siegel--Walfisz, Bombieri--Vinogradov, Bombieri--Davenport--Halberstam,
Elliott--Halberstam or GRH, at any polynomial level.  Integer majorants
that are $\geq1$ only on $\A\cap\{(n,P(z))=1\}$, $z\leq N^A$, are majorants of
$\A(\cG\cup\{0\bmod p:p\leq z\})$; so Theorem~\ref{thm:main} covers the setting of
Theorem~\ref{thm:primemaj}(2) for all mixtures \cite[\S4.4]{PL}.  Under GRH
signed evaluation does not help either \cite[Prop.~4.3]{PL}
\lab{conditional} on GRH: if the moduli of $\nu$ are $\leq N^A$,
$\sum_i|a_i|\leq N^{1/2-\varepsilon}$ and $\nu(p)\geq0$ off a set $F$ of $\leq N^{1/2}$ primes,
then $\sum_{p\leq N,\,p\notin F}\nu(p)\geq(1-o(1))\mathrm{li}(N)\E^*\nu$.  Unconditional error
terms cannot resolve main terms of size $\pi(N)\e^{-(\log N)^{3/4}}$ at all
\cite[\S6.3]{LS2} (\emph{Assessment}).

\begin{proposition}[finite range; {\cite[Prop.~6.1]{LS2}}; \lab{proved}]\label{prop:finiterange}
For a prime $q\in(N,2N]$, $\nu=\sum_{p\leq N,\,p\in\A}\ind[n\equiv p\pmod q]$ is $\geq0$ on $\Z$,
$\geq1$ at the primes of $\A\cap[1,N]$, of level $\leq\log2N$ and coefficient sum
$\leq N$, and $\sum_{p\leq N}\nu(p)=\#\{p\leq N:p\in\A\}$.
\end{proposition}
So no cap of any kind holds if $\nu\geq1$ is required only there; what is
missing is the location of those primes, which is non-CRT input.  The other relaxation, $\nu(p)\geq0$ only for $p\leq N$ but $\nu\geq1$ at
all primes of $\A$, is \lab{open}; that certifying it needs the location of
the primes $\leq N$ is an \emph{Assessment}.  Both relaxations reduce to
the prime-majorant condition when every reduced class mod $L$ contains a
prime $\leq N$, so the gap lives at $L>N^c$ \cite[\S6]{PL}.

\subsection{Interval counts with bounded frequency denominators}
By \eqref{eq:signed}, a direct count of $\sum_{n\leq N}\nu(n)$ may exploit
cancellation between frequencies.  If all denominators are below $N$, it
cannot.

\begin{theorem}[Selberg minorant; {\cite[Thm~2.2]{IF}}; \lab{proved}]\label{thm:IFminorant}
Let $\nu\geq0$ on $\Z$ be periodic, with every nonzero frequency of
denominator $\leq D$.  Then $\sum_{n=1}^N\nu(n)\geq(N-D)\E\nu$ for every $N\geq1$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Let $I=[1/2,N+1/2]$ and $\delta=1/D$.  Selberg's minorant \cite{Vaaler} is an
integrable $F:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R$ with $F\leq\ind_I$, $\hat F$ continuous and
supported in $[-\delta,\delta]$, and $\hat F(0)=|I|-\delta^{-1}=N-D$.  $F$ is entire of
exponential type $2\pi\delta$ and integrable, so $\sum_n|F(n)|<\infty$, and Poisson
summation for $F(x)\e(x\theta)$ gives $\sum_nF(n)\e(n\theta)=\sum_k\hat F(k-\theta)$ for every
$\theta$.  If $\theta\notin\Z$ has denominator $q\leq D$, then $\|\theta\|\geq1/q\geq\delta$, so every
term vanishes ($\hat F$ is continuous and $0$ outside $(-\delta,\delta)$).  For $\theta\in\Z$
the sum is $\hat F(0)$.  Since $\nu$ is a finite trigonometric sum,
$\sum_nF(n)\nu(n)=\sum_\theta\hat\nu(\theta)\sum_nF(n)\e(n\theta)=(N-D)\E\nu$.  Finally
$F(n)\leq\ind_{[1,N]}(n)$ and $\nu(n)\geq0$ for every $n\in\Z$, so
$\sum_{n\leq N}\nu(n)\geq\sum_nF(n)\nu(n)$.
\end{proof}
Positivity is used at every integer (Remark~\ref{rem:positivity}).  The
hypothesis holds iff $\nu$ is a combination of classes of moduli $\leq D$.

\begin{corollary}[{\cite[Cor.~2.3]{IF}}, {\cite[\S4.3]{KA3}}; \lab{proved} via Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}; Case A as there]\label{cor:IFcap}
Let $\nu$ be a majorant of $\A(\cG)$ that is a combination of classes of
moduli $\leq N/2$, with any coefficients.  Then
$\sum_{n\leq N}\nu(n)\geq(N/2)\exp\{-C(\log N)^{3/4}\}$ for $N$ large, however the
interval sum is evaluated.  No bound on the family primes is needed.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
$\nu$ has level $\leq\log N$; apply Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}, then
Theorem~\ref{thm:IFminorant} with $D=N/2$.
\end{proof}
This caps $\Lambda^2$ and $\beta$-sieves of level $<N/2$ with any remainder
evaluation (dispersion, Kloosterman sums, Erd\H{o}s--Tur\'an/Vaaler or
floor/ceiling rounding, the exact $|S_N(\theta)|$, smooth windows
$\Phi(n/N)\geq\ind_{[1,N]}(n)$), with no need for $(H_{\rm eq})$.  Inter-frequency gain
must come from classes of combined modulus $>N/2$.

\begin{theorem}[coefficient budget; {\cite[Lemma~2.4, Thm~2.5, Rem.~2.6]{IF}}; \lab{proved} via Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}; Case A as there]\label{thm:IFbudget}
Let every prime of $\cG$ be $\leq N^A$, let $\nu=\sum_ia_i\ind[n\equiv b_i\pmod{d_i}]$ be
a majorant of $\A(\cG)$, and $T_>=\sum_{d_i>N/2}|a_i|$.  If $B=N\e^{-s}$ satisfies
$B\geq\sum_{n\leq N}\nu(n)$ and $T_>\leq cB$, then $s\leq\log(2+12c)+C_A(\log N)^{3/4}$
for $N\geq N_0(A)$, with $N_0,C_A$ independent of $c$.  If
$T_>\leq(N/24)\e^{-C_A(\log N)^{3/4}}$, the saving is $\leq C_A(\log N)^{3/4}+\log4$
whatever $B$ is.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
For $F$ as above with $D=N/2$, Poisson along a progression gives
$|\sum_{n\equiv b\,(d)}F(n)-(N-D)/d|\leq2(N+D)/D=6$, and $0$ if $d\leq D$.  So
$B\geq\sum_nF(n)\nu(n)\geq(N/2)\E\nu-6T_>$, i.e.\ $\E\nu\leq(2+12c)\e^{-s}$.  For the mean,
follow the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:main}: the coarsening of
Lemma~\ref{lem:budget} touches only terms of level $>\log(N/2)$, whose
coefficient sum is $\leq T_>$.
\end{proof}
$T_>$ may be replaced by its infimum over representations of $\nu$; $c=1$
contains the class of Theorem~\ref{thm:main}.  Not covered: \emph{hybrid}
methods that evaluate the classes of modulus $\leq N/2$ in any way and
charge each larger class its trivial count.  Their bound
$\sum_{n\leq N}\nu+\sum_{d_i>N/2}(|a_i|-a_ir_i)$, with
$r_i=\#\{n\leq N:n\equiv b_i\ (d_i)\}-N/d_i$, can be far below $T_>$.  This is a
proof gap \lab{open}, not a counterexample \cite[Rem.~2.6]{IF}.  What is
left is to count integers $n\leq N$ with prescribed witness sets of combined
modulus $>N/2$ better than termwise (\emph{Assessment}).

\subsection{Hybrid methods}\label{sec:hybrid}
The hybrid gap just stated is narrowed, but not closed, in \cite{IF2}
(review \cite{IF2rev}: all claims sound; one defect, a Farkas argument on
truncated LPs, was repaired by a periodic LP).  Fix $N$, a majorant
$\nu=\sum_ia_i\ind[n\equiv b_i\pmod{d_i}]$ of $\A(\cG)$, and split the terms into
\emph{small} ($d_i\leq N/2$) and \emph{large} ($d_i>N/2$).  Put
$c(b,d)=\#\{n\leq N:n\equiv b\ (d)\}\in\{\lfloor N/d\rfloor,\lceil N/d\rceil\}$.  A
\emph{hybrid method} \cite[Def.~1.2]{IF2} bounds the small part by any valid
upper bound for its interval sum and charges a large term the least
position-blind amount $\beta^*(a,d)=a\lceil N/d\rceil$ ($a>0$), $a\lfloor N/d\rfloor$ ($a<0$);
call the resulting bound $B_{\rm hyb}(\nu)$.  The charge
$aN/d+|a|$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:IFbudget} dominates $\beta^*$.  A large class is
\emph{full} if $c(b,d)=\lceil N/d\rceil$ and \emph{sparse} if $c(b,d)=\lfloor N/d\rfloor$.

\begin{lemma}[sign rule; {\cite[Lemma~3.1, Ex.~3.2]{IF2}}; \lab{proved}]\label{lem:signrule}
For every representation,
$B_{\rm hyb}(\nu)\geq\sum_{n\leq N}\nu(n)+\sum_{i\ \text{wrong}}|a_i|$, with equality when the
small part is counted exactly; a large term is \emph{wrong-signed} if
$a_i>0$ on a sparse, not full class, or $a_i<0$ on a full, not sparse class.
Right-signed large terms are counted exactly.  At $N=20$ the class
$\ind[0\bmod21]$ has a representation (twelve full classes modulo $21$ and
classes modulo $3$ and $7$) with hybrid charge $0$.  Hence no inequality
$B_{\rm hyb}(\nu)\geq cN\E\nu$, $c>0$, holds for all $\nu\geq0$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
$\beta^*(a,d)-a\,c(b,d)$ equals $a(\lceil N/d\rceil-c)$ for $a>0$ and $|a|(c-\lfloor N/d\rfloor)$
for $a<0$, and $c\in\{\lfloor N/d\rfloor,\lceil N/d\rceil\}$ with the two differing by at most
$1$.  The example is checked by direct computation \cite[\S3]{IF2}.
\end{proof}
So the Selberg accounting of Theorem~\ref{thm:IFbudget} does not extend to
hybrids as it stands.  It does when the \emph{free} large mass
(right-signed terms) is small: if the positive terms on classes of
modulus $>N$ meeting $[1,N]$, plus the right-signed mass at moduli in
$(N/2,N]$, are $\leq cB$, the saving is at most $S_A+\log(2+12(1+c))$, where
$S_A$ is the cap of Theorem~\ref{thm:main} \cite[Cor.~5.1]{IF2} \lab{proved}.
Large free mass is not in itself a counterexample:
$(1+K)-K\sum_{b\bmod N}\ind[b\bmod N]\equiv1$ has $B=N$ and right-signed mass $KN$.

\begin{hypothesis}[Flat$(C,t,s_0,\Delta)$ at $N$; {\cite[\S5]{IF2}}; \lab{open}]\label{hyp:Flat}
There is a summable $F:\Z\to\mathbb R$ with $F\leq\ind_{[1,N]}$; $F(s)=tN/d$ for every
class $s$ of modulus $d\leq N/2$ (where $F(s)=\sum_{n\in s}F(n)$); $F(s)\geq tN/d+s_0$
for every class of modulus $d>CN$ meeting $[1,N]$, and $F(s)\geq tN/d-1$ for
those missing it; and $|F(s)-tN/d|\leq\Delta$ for every class of modulus in
$(N/2,CN]$.
\end{hypothesis}
Unlike Selberg's minorant, which is $\approx0$ near the ends of $[1,N]$, such
an $F$ is \emph{flat}: for large moduli it is $\geq s_0$ on $[1,N]$ and $\geq-1$
off it.

\begin{theorem}[conditional hybrid cap; {\cite[Thm~5.2]{IF2}}; \lab{proved} implication, \lab{conditional} on Flat; Case A as in Theorem~\ref{thm:main}]\label{thm:hybridcap}
Assume Flat$(C,t,s_0,\Delta)$ at $N$ with $t,1/\Delta\geq\e^{-S_A}$ and $s_0\geq N^{-A_1}$.  If a
hybrid bound $B$ for $\A(\cG)$, family primes $\leq N^A$, has right-signed
mass at moduli in $(N/2,CN]$ at most $cB$, then for $N\geq N_0(A,A_1,C)$
\[
 \log(N/B)\leq S'+\log\frac{1+\Delta(1+c)}t,\qquad S'=C_{A,A_1,C}(\log N)^{3/4}(\log\log N)^{3/4}.
\]
Terms of modulus $>CN$, wrong-signed terms and free negative terms are
unrestricted.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
By $\nu\geq0$, $F\leq\ind_{[1,N]}$ and Lemma~\ref{lem:signrule},
$B\geq\sum_nF(n)\nu(n)+T_{\rm wr}$, and the small part gives $tN\E\nu_{\rm small}$.
Term by term, the flatness conditions make each large term of modulus
$>CN$ contribute $\geq0$ beyond $tN/d_i\cdot a_i$, and each term of modulus in
$(N/2,CN]$ at least $-\Delta|a_i|$ (right-signed) or $(1-\Delta)|a_i|$ (wrong).
Hence $(1+\Delta)B\geq tN\E\nu-\Delta T_{\rm mid}+s_0W^+_{>CN}$, with $W^+_{>CN}$ the mass of
positive terms of modulus $>CN$ meeting $[1,N]$.  Unless $(1+\Delta(1+c))B\geq tN$
(then the bound is trivial), this gives $\E\nu<1$ and total mass $\leq N^{A_1+1}+N$ on
terms of modulus $>CN$, which bounds the level after the coarsening of
Lemma~\ref{lem:budget}; the mean is then capped as in Theorem~\ref{thm:main}
\cite[\S5]{IF2}.
\end{proof}
\cite{IF2} uses the cap of \cite[Thm~5.1]{KA2} with the case analysis of
\cite[Cor.~6.1]{KA2}, hence the factor $(\log\log N)^{3/4}$.  The cap enters only
as a black-box bound on $\log(1/\E\nu)$ at a level $\leq(A+A_1+2)\log N+S+\lambda_0$, so
Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap} and the case analysis of Theorem~\ref{thm:main} give
$S'=C_{A,A_1,C}(\log N)^{3/4}$ (observation of this note, pointer-level, not
reviewed).  A variant
\cite[Thm~5.2$'$]{IF2} needs right-signed mass only on $(N,CN]$, under a
stronger Flat with sign conditions on $(N/2,N]$ \lab{proved} implication.

Flat reduces to a purely combinatorial statement with no arithmetic and
no $\A$.

\begin{hypothesis}[SPW$(C,\sigma,\Delta_0)$ at $N$, spread pseudo-window; {\cite[\S9]{IF2}}; \lab{open}]\label{hyp:SPW}
There is a summable $R\geq0$ on $\Z$ with $R(s)=c(s)$ for every class $s$ of
modulus $\leq N/2$; $R(s)\leq1-\sigma$ for every class of modulus $>CN$; and
$|R(s)-c(s)|\leq\Delta_0$ for every class of modulus in $(N/2,CN]$.
\end{hypothesis}
That is, a random integer that looks exactly like a uniform point of
$[1,N]$ modulo every $d\leq N/2$, but which no class of modulus $>CN$ sees as a
point of $[1,N]$.  The window $\ind_{[1,N]}$ itself fails only the second
condition.

\begin{proposition}[{\cite[Prop.~9.1, Lemma~9.3]{IF2}}; \lab{proved}]\label{prop:SPW}
(1) SPW$(C,\sigma,\Delta_0)$ implies Flat$(C,t,s_0,\Delta)$ with $s_0=\sigma/2$,
$t=\theta/2$, $\Delta=6\theta+\Delta_0$, $\theta=\sigma/(2(\sigma+\tau+1/(2C)))$, $\tau$ an
absolute constant, via $F=\theta F_S+(1-\theta)(\ind_{[1,N]}-R)$ with $F_S$
Selberg's minorant of Theorem~\ref{thm:IFminorant} at $\delta=2/N$.  So SPW at
every large $N$, with $\sigma$ bounded below, gives the cap of
Theorem~\ref{thm:hybridcap} for hybrids with controlled right-signed mass on
$(N/2,CN]$ \lab{conditional}.
(2) Every SPW has $\sigma\leq\sigma_C(N)=\min_{q\leq N/2}(1-\lceil N/q\rceil/k_q)$,
$k_q=\lfloor CN/q\rfloor+1$; this is $2/5$ at $C=2$ in the limit, and SPW is
impossible for $C\leq1$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(2) Take $b$ with $c(b,q)=\lceil N/q\rceil$.  The class $b\bmod q$ is the
disjoint union of the $k_q$ classes $b+jq\bmod k_qq$, of modulus $k_qq>CN$;
each has $R$-mass $\leq1-\sigma$, and they sum to $c(b,q)$.  (1) is a direct
check of the Flat conditions, using the decay of $F_S$ off $[1,N]$ to bound
$\tau$ \cite[\S9]{IF2}.
\end{proof}
LPs find SPW at $C=2$ for $N\leq60$, attaining $\sigma_C(N)$ with long enough
support, and Flat at sampled $N\leq100$ \lab{evidence} (floating-point LPs).
Open: SPW itself, and right-signed mass at moduli in $(N,CN]$; periodic
certificates show $(N/2,N]$ cannot be freed at $C=1.5$
\cite[\S10]{IF2} \lab{evidence}.  No hybrid beating $3/4$ is known.

\section{Tuple counts and truncated weights}\label{sec:TU}

Among the non-CRT inputs of Section~\ref{sec:excluded}, item~5, the most
natural is tuple counting: aggregate control of witness correlations of
growing order, with combined moduli far above $N$.  The sources are
\cite{TU} and \cite{TU2}, whose reviews \cite{TUrev,TU2rev} found them sound
after minor repairs (incorporated), and \cite[\S\S5, 7]{KA3}.  The review
\cite{KA3rev} covers \cite[\S5]{KA3}; \cite[\S7]{KA3} (Lemma~\ref{lem:packing}
and the Assessment closing Section~\ref{sec:TUtrunc}) was added later and
checked by the coordinator only.  Nothing here gives $\theta>3/4$; the section
locates what a tuple-count proof of $\theta>3/4$ would have to contain.

\subsection{Witness correlations and the tuple-count hypothesis}
Let $\cP_y$ be the primes $\ell\leq y$ with $\ell\equiv3\pmod4$, $A_\ell=(\ell+1)/4$,
$F(\ell)=|\cR(\ell)|$ and $p_\ell=F(\ell)/\ell$.  The \emph{prime family} consists
of the classes of $\cR(\ell)$, $\ell\in\cP_y$; its \emph{witness count} is
$f_y(n)=\sum_{\ell\in\cP_y}\ind[n\bmod\ell\in\cR(\ell)]$.  Under the CRT law the
summands are independent $\Bern(p_\ell)$, and
$c(\log y)^2\leq\mu_y:=\sum_{\ell\in\cP_y}p_\ell\leq C(\log y)^2$ for $y\geq3$
\cite[(1.3)]{TU}.  Put
\[
 S_j(N)=\sum_{n\leq N}\binom{f_y(n)}j=\sum_{|T|=j}C_T(N),\qquad e_j=e_j(p)=\sum_{|T|=j}\delta_T,
\]
where $T$ runs over $j$-sets of classes with distinct primes, $C_T(N)$
counts the $n\leq N$ in all classes of $T$, and $\delta_T=\prod_{\ell\in T}\ell^{-1}$.
Trivially $|S_j(N)-Ne_j|\leq\#\{T\}$ \cite[Lemma~1.2]{TU}.  A tuple with
$\prod_T\ell>N$ has $C_T(N)\in\{0,1\}$ while $N\delta_T<1$; every tuple-count
hypothesis beyond the trivial range is about such tuples.

\begin{lemma}[shift form; {\cite[Lemma~1.3]{TU}}; \lab{proved}]\label{lem:shiftform}
For $\ell\in\cP_y$ let $\mathcal D_\ell$ be a set of divisors of $A_\ell^2$ whose
residues $-4D\bmod\ell$ are the classes of $\cR(\ell)$, each once, and put
$\omega_{y,D}(m)=\#\{\ell\in\cP_y:D\in\mathcal D_\ell,\ \ell\mid m\}$.  Then
$f_y(n)=\sum_{D\geq1}\omega_{y,D}(n+4D)$ for all $n\in\Z$, and
$S_k(N)=\sum\#\{n\leq N:\ \ell_i\mid n+4D_i,\ i\leq k\}$, summed over $k$-sets
$\{(\ell_i,D_i)\}$ with distinct $\ell_i\in\cP_y$ and $D_i\in\mathcal D_{\ell_i}$.  All
shifts satisfy $4D\leq(y+1)^2/4$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The classes $-4D$, $D\in\mathcal D_\ell$, are distinct modulo $\ell$, so
$\ind[n\bmod\ell\in\cR(\ell)]=\sum_{D\in\mathcal D_\ell}\ind[\ell\mid n+4D]$; regroup by $D$.
Expanding $\binom{f_y(n)}k$ counts $k$-sets of hit primes, each with its
unique $D$; and $D\leq A_\ell^2$.
\end{proof}
So $S_k$ is the distinct-prime part of $k$-point correlations of
$\omega$-type functions along up to $k$ shifts $4D$, with $\prod\ell_i\gg N$.

For even $K$ let $y_K$ be the largest $y$ with $\mu_y\leq K/\e^2$, so
$\log y_K\asymp K^{1/2}$, and put $\eta_K=\e^{-K/\e^2}/K$.  Write $\mathrm{TC}(N;K,y,\eta)$
for: $|S_j(N)-Ne_j|\leq\eta N$ for all $1\leq j\leq K$.

\begin{hypothesis}[$\mathrm{TC}_\theta$; {\cite[\S2]{TU}}; \lab{open}]\label{hyp:TC}
Let $\theta\in(0,1)$ and $K_N=2\lceil(\log N)^\theta\rceil$.  For all large $N$,
$\mathrm{TC}(N;K_N,y_{K_N},\eta_{K_N})$ holds.
\end{hypothesis}
It is proved for $\theta<2/3$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:TCrange}); for
$\theta\in(2/3,1)$ it needs a compensating excess
(Corollary~\ref{cor:TCexcess}) and is expected false (Assessment there).

\begin{theorem}[{\cite[Thm~2.1, Cors~2.2, 2.3]{TU}}; \lab{proved}; the bound on $E(N)$ \lab{conditional} on Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:TC}]\label{thm:TCbrun}
If $K$ is even and $\mathrm{TC}(N;K,y,\eta)$ holds, then
$\#\{n\leq N:f_y(n)=0\}\leq N(\prod_{\ell\in\cP_y}(1-p_\ell)+e_K+K\eta)$.  For $y=y_K$,
$\eta=\eta_K$ this is $\leq(\e+2)N\e^{-K/\e^2}$.  Hence Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:TC}
implies $E(N)\leq(\e+2)N\exp\{-(2/\e^2)(\log N)^\theta\}$ for large $N$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
For integers $f\geq1$, $\sum_{j=0}^K(-1)^j\binom fj=(-1)^K\binom{f-1}K$; for $f=0$ the
sum is $1$.  For even $K$ it is $\geq\ind[f=0]$.  Summing over $n\leq N$,
$\#\{f_y=0\}\leq\sum_j(-1)^jS_j\leq N\sum_j(-1)^je_j+K\eta N$, and under the CRT law
the identity gives $\sum_j(-1)^je_j\leq\prod(1-p_\ell)+\E\binom{f}{K}=\prod(1-p_\ell)+e_K$.
For $y=y_K$: each $p_\ell<1$, so $\mu_{y_K}>K/\e^2-1$ by maximality, $\prod(1-p_\ell)<\e^{1-K/\e^2}$,
$e_K\leq(\e\mu/K)^K\leq\e^{-K}$ and $K\eta_K=\e^{-K/\e^2}$.  The classes are forced.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[trivial range and squares; {\cite[Props~2.4, 4.2]{TU}}; \lab{proved}]\label{prop:TCrange}
(1) $\mathrm{TC}(N;K,y_K,\eta_K)$ holds for $K\leq c_0(\log N)^{2/3}$; so
Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:TC} holds for $\theta<2/3$.  (2) It is false for even
$K\geq(\e^2/2+\varepsilon)\log N$, $N\geq N_0(\varepsilon)$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(1) The termwise bound gives
$|S_j-Ne_j|\leq(\sum_\ell F(\ell))^K\leq(Cy_K(\log y_K)^2)^K\leq N\eta_K$ once
$K^{3/2}\ll\log N$; this is Brun's pure sieve.  (2) By
Lemma~\ref{lem:mordell} every class of $\cR(\ell)$ is a non-residue, and
$0\notin\cR(\ell)$.  So squares avoid the family, $\#\{f_y=0\}\geq\lfloor\sqrt N\rfloor$,
against the bound $(\e+2)N\e^{-K/\e^2}\leq(\e+2)N^{1/2-\varepsilon'}$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:TCbrun}.
\end{proof}
For $\theta>2/3$ the content of Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:TC} is aggregate
cancellation among tuples of combined modulus
$\exp\{(\log N)^{3\theta/2+o(1)}\}$, each prime $\leq y_K=N^{o(1)}$; no total-variation
(Kubilius-type) model exists there \cite[Prop.~4.1]{TU} \lab{proved}.

\subsection{Bounded order cannot pass \texorpdfstring{$3/4$}{3/4}}
Take a prime-slice system (Definition~\ref{def:slice}) with $p_\ell(c)\leq1/4$
and $|F_\ell(c)|<\ell$ for all $\ell\in\cP$, $c\in R$, and for a term of $\nu$ put
$T_i=\{\ell\in\cP:\ell\mid d_i\}$.

\begin{definition}[mixed majorants; {\cite[Def.~3.0]{TU}}]\label{def:mixed}
$\nu$ is \emph{$(\lambda_0,k)$-mixed} if every term has level
$\sum_{T_i}\log\ell\leq\lambda_0$ or \emph{(prime) order} $|T_i|\leq k$.
\end{definition}
A $k$-point correlation of slice classes has order $\leq k$ whatever its
modulus; so $P\circ f$ with $\deg P\leq k$ and the majorant of
Theorem~\ref{thm:TCbrun} with $K=k$, plus any terms of level $\leq\lambda_0$, are mixed.

\begin{theorem}[truncated weights on prime slices; {\cite[Thm~3.1]{TU}}; \lab{proved}]\label{thm:truncslice}
Let $\nu$ be $(\lambda_0,k)$-mixed, $L_0>0$, $s_\ell=\min(\log\ell,L_0)$ and
$\lambda=\max(\lambda_0,kL_0)$.  Then \eqref{eq:slice} holds with this $\lambda$, with
$s_*=\min_\cP s_\ell$, with $\bar\mu=\sum_{\ell\in\cP}\bar p_\ell$, and with the mass sum
replaced by $\sum_{\ell\in\cP}\bar p_\ell\e^{-\alpha s_\ell}$, i.e.\ by
$\sum_{\log\ell\leq L_0}\bar p_\ell\ell^{-\alpha}+\e^{-\alpha L_0}\sum_{\log\ell>L_0}\bar p_\ell$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Prove Theorem~\ref{thm:slice} with Proposition~\ref{prop:boolean} applied
to the weights $s_\ell$, which it allows.  A term has weighted level
$\sum_{T_i}s_\ell\leq\min(\sum_{T_i}\log\ell,|T_i|L_0)\leq\lambda$.  As $s_\ell\leq\lambda$ for all $\ell$,
no coordinate is discarded, so $p_\ell(c)\leq1/4$ is needed for every slice
prime; it is assumed.  The weights do not depend on $c$, so the Jensen
step is unchanged.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[order needed; {\cite[Cors~3.2, 3.3]{TU}}; \lab{proved}; Case A \lab{proved mod} Elsholtz--Tao Prop.~1.4]\label{cor:orderslice}
For the families of Corollary~\ref{cor:sliceCap} with slice primes $\leq N^A$,
every $(A\log N,k)$-mixed majorant has
$\log(1/\E\nu)\leq C_9(A,C)[(\log N)^{3/4}+k\log\log N]+\log(P/\varphi(P))$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Take $\lambda_0=A\log N=\lambda$, $L_0=\lambda_0/k$, $\alpha=\max(\lambda_0^{-1/4},3k\log\lambda_0/\lambda_0)$.  As in
Corollary~\ref{cor:sliceCap}, the profile is $\ll\min(\alpha,1)^{-3}$ and
$\bar\mu\ll\lambda_0^3$.  So $19\alpha\lambda\leq19\lambda_0^{3/4}+57k\log\lambda_0$, the first mass sum is
$\ll\lambda_0^{3/4}$, the second is $O(1)$ as $\alpha L_0\geq3\log\lambda_0$, and the
$G$-terms are $O(\log^2(k+\log N))$.
\end{proof}
Call a method \emph{CRT-main-term} if its bound for $\#(\A\cap[1,N])$ is
$\geq\frac12N\E\nu$, i.e.\ its evaluation of $\sum_{n\leq N}\nu(n)$ asserts the CRT
main term up to half of it.  Hypotheses ``order-$j$ correlation sums equal
their CRT values up to a small error'', $j\leq k$, are of this kind; a
low-level part evaluated exactly with a large favourable deficit is not.
For such methods (and $\log(P/\varphi(P))=o((\log N)^\theta)$) bounded order gives no
$\theta>3/4$, and a saving $(\log N)^\theta$ needs order $k\geq c(\log N)^\theta/\log\log N$
\lab{proved}.  Order $2\lceil(\log N)^\theta\rceil$ suffices under
Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:TC} (Theorem~\ref{thm:TCbrun}) \lab{conditional}.

\begin{corollary}[class order, all four types; {\cite[Cor.~3.4]{TU}}, {\cite[\S4.3]{KA3}}; \lab{proved}; Case A \lab{proved mod} Elsholtz--Tao Prop.~1.4, Thm~7.1, Cor.~7.4, (7.10)]\label{cor:classorder}
Let $\cG$ be as in Definition~\ref{def:types}, and let every term of a
majorant $\nu$ of $\A(\cG)$ be an intersection of at most $k$ residue classes
of modulus $\leq N^A$, or have level $\leq A\log N$.  Then
$\log(1/\E\nu)\leq C(kA\log N)^{3/4}$ for $N\geq N_0(A)$.  So for CRT-main-term
methods a saving $(\log N)^\theta$ needs $k\geq c(\log N)^{4\theta/3-1}$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Such an intersection is empty or one class whose modulus divides the
product of the $k$ moduli; its level is $\leq kA\log N$.  Apply
Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}.
\end{proof}
(\cite{TU} had the factor $(\log(kA\log N))^{3/4}$ of the previous cap.)  The
window of $k$ between $(\log N)^{4\theta/3-1}$ and $(\log N)^\theta/\log\log N$ is
treated next.

\begin{proposition}[fixed shifts; {\cite[Prop.~4.3 and extension]{TU}}; \lab{proved}]\label{prop:fixedshift}
Let $\mathcal S$ be a set of $r$ positive integers.  Let $\nu\geq0$ be a finite
combination of intersections of the events $M\mid n+4D$ with $D\in\mathcal S$,
$M\equiv3\pmod4$, $D\mid A_M^2$, $M\leq N^A$, and let $\nu\geq1$ on the avoider set
of a forced-class family containing these classes.  Then
$\E\nu\geq\exp\{-2r(\log\log N^A+3)\}$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
$\nu=G(\text{indicator vector})$.  The avoider set is nonempty ($0\notin\cR(M)$
as $(D,M)=1$) and has vector $0$, so $G(0)\geq1$.  A hit $M\mid n+4D$ gives a
prime $q\mid n+4D$, $q\equiv3\pmod4$, $q\leq N^A$, $q\nmid D$.  So $\E\nu\geq\prod_q(1-r_q/q)$,
$r_q\leq\min(r,q-1)$; use $1-x\geq\e^{-2x}$ for $q>2r$ and $1-r_q/q\geq1/q$ for $q\leq2r$.
\end{proof}
So witness information of any order and precision along $r$ fixed shifts
saves $\leq2r(\log\log N+O(1))$ for CRT-main-term methods.  Known
divisor-correlation theorems (Heath-Brown, Deshouillers--Iwaniec,
Matom\"aki--Radziwi\l\l--Tao, Tao--Ter\"av\"ainen) have fixed or averaged
shifts; Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:TC} needs $\asymp K$ shifts jointly.  That no known
theorem or standard conjecture supplies it for any $\theta>2/3$ is an
\emph{Assessment} \cite[Ass.~4.4]{TU}.  Not covered: non-witness
information along the shifts ($q\equiv1\pmod4$, $\tau(n+4D)$, primes $>N^A$), and
combinations over all shift tuples.

\subsection{Truncated weights}\label{sec:TUtrunc}
Theorem~\ref{thm:trunc} and Corollaries~\ref{cor:primeorder}
and~\ref{cor:boundedr} carry the proviso of Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}: proved
given \cite{KA,KA2} as reviewed; Case A \lab{proved mod} Elsholtz--Tao
Prop.~1.4, Thm~7.1, Cor.~7.4, (7.10).

\begin{definition}[{\cite[Def.~5.0]{KA3}}]\label{def:trunc}
Fix $L_0>0$ and put $s_\ell=\min(\log\ell,L_0)$ for primes $\ell>W$.  A term
$a\ind[n\equiv b\pmod d]$ has \emph{truncated level} $\sum_{\ell\mid d,\,\ell>W}s_\ell$ and
\emph{prime order} $\omega_{>W}(d)$; $\nu$ is \emph{$(\lambda_0,k)$-mixed} if every term
has level $\leq\lambda_0$ or prime order $\leq k$.  With $L_0=\lambda_0/k$ such a $\nu$
has truncated level $\leq\lambda_0$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[truncated-weight cap; {\cite[Thm~5.1]{KA3}}; \lab{proved}; Case A \lab{proved mod} Elsholtz--Tao Prop.~1.4, Thm~7.1, Cor.~7.4, (7.10)]\label{thm:trunc}
Let $W,\lambda_0,C$ be as in Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}, $K_3'$ as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:firstnoB}, and $C_0$ as in Corollary~\ref{cor:kmean}.  Let
$\cG$ be as in Definition~\ref{def:types} with all family primes $\leq\e^\Lambda$,
and let $\nu\geq0$ on $\Z$, $\nu\geq1$ on $\A(\cG)$, have truncated level $\leq\lambda$,
with $\lambda_0\leq\lambda\leq\Lambda$ and $L_0\leq\lambda$.  With $d_0=\lfloor\lambda/L_0\rfloor$,
\[
 \log\frac1{\E\nu}\leq C\lambda^{3/4}+2d_0\log\frac{C_0(K_3'\Lambda^3+4d_0)}{d_0}+\frac83d_0+\log(22d_0+22)+6.
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Theorem~\ref{thm:wseq} holds for any class $\mathcal F$ of functions closed
under conditioning on earlier coordinates, if (S$_w$) holds on $\mathcal F$.
Take for $\mathcal F$ the sums of terms each depending on a prime set $T$ with
$\sum_Ts_\ell\leq\lambda$; conditioning only shrinks $T$.  The cost ledger, in
increasing order of primes: (i) singletons $W<\ell\leq\e^{\min(s_1,L_0)}$,
$s_1=\lambda^{1/4}$, and blocks $\{2^is_1<\log\ell\leq\min(2^{i+1}s_1,L_0,\lambda/2)\}$, where
$s_\ell=\log\ell$ and the depths are those of Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}: total
$\leq C\lambda^{3/4}+O(\log^2\lambda)$; (ii) if $L_0>\lambda/2$, the linear block
$(\e^{\lambda/2},\e^{L_0}]$: $\leq2\log(1+3\e^{-\lambda/4})$; (iii) one \emph{top block}
$V_{\rm top}=\{\log\ell>L_0\}$ of any width, where $s_\ell=L_0$, so every
$f\in\mathcal F$ is $d_0$-local.  Theorem~\ref{thm:kcomp} is arithmetic-free,
and $\E m_{V_{\rm top}}\leq\mathfrak M(\e^\Lambda)\leq K_3'\Lambda^3$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:firstnoB}).
Corollary~\ref{cor:kmean} with $t$ chosen per history, and Jensen, give
$\E\Phi_{\rm top}\leq d_0\log(C_0(K_3'\Lambda^3+4d_0)/d_0)+\frac43d_0+\frac12\log(22d_0+22)+3$,
doubled by Theorem~\ref{thm:wseq}; (iv) base and Jensen: $2W+\log2$.  The
leak bound of Lemma~\ref{lem:secondnoB} is per prime, so $\mathfrak L\leq1/2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[prime order; {\cite[Cor.~5.2]{KA3}}; \lab{proved}; Case A \lab{proved mod} Elsholtz--Tao Prop.~1.4, Thm~7.1, Cor.~7.4, (7.10)]\label{cor:primeorder}
Let $\cG$ be as in Definition~\ref{def:types} with family primes $\leq N^A$,
and let every term of a majorant $\nu$ of $\A(\cG)$ have level $\leq A\log N$ or
prime order $\leq k$, $1\leq k\leq(A\log N)^3$.  Then
$\log(1/\E\nu)\leq C_A[(\log N)^{3/4}+k\log\log N]$.  So a CRT-main-term
saving $(\log N)^\theta$, $\theta>3/4$, needs prime order $k\geq c_A(\log N)^\theta/\log\log N$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Project onto the lcm of the family moduli, as in the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:main}; prime sets shrink, so both term types are kept,
and all primes are $\leq\e^\Lambda$, $\Lambda=A\log N$.  For $k=1$ use
Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}; for $k\geq2$, Theorem~\ref{thm:trunc} with $\lambda=\Lambda$,
$L_0=\lambda/k$, $d_0=k$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[boundedly many large primes; {\cite[Cor.~5.3]{KA3}}; \lab{proved}; Case A \lab{proved mod} Elsholtz--Tao Prop.~1.4, Thm~7.1, Cor.~7.4, (7.10)]\label{cor:boundedr}
If every modulus of $\cG$ has at most $r$ prime factors $>W$, an intersection
of $k$ classes of $\cG$ has prime order $\leq kr$; so the corresponding mixed
majorants have $\log(1/\E\nu)\leq C_A[(\log N)^{3/4}+kr\log\log N]$.
\end{corollary}
\emph{Scope.}  $W$ is fixed, not a moving cutoff: the twin moduli of
\cite{TW2,TW4} and the atoms $\cR(k\ell)$ of \cite{TQ} may have cofactors with many
primes above $W$, and are then covered by Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap} but not
by Corollary~\ref{cor:boundedr}.

\begin{proposition}[block-sparse families; {\cite[Prop.~7.1]{TU2}}; \lab{proved} relative to {\cite[Thm~5.1]{KA2}}; Case A \lab{proved mod} Elsholtz--Tao Prop.~1.4]\label{prop:blocksparse}
Let all moduli of $\cG$ be $\leq N^A$, each with at most $r$ prime factors in
every interval $(x,x^2]$, $x\geq2$.  If every term of $\nu$ has level
$\leq\lambda_0=A\log N$ or is an intersection of at most $k$ classes of $\cG$, then
$\log(1/\E\nu)\leq C\lambda_0^{3/4}(\log\lambda_0)^{3/4}+Ckr(\log\log N)^2$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
The level enters \cite[Thm~5.1]{KA2} only through $d$-locality, and now
$d\leq\lambda_0/s+kr$ in $(\e^s,\e^{2s}]$.  The step cost $d\log(C_0(M+4d)/d)$ is
subadditive, so the $kr$ part costs $\ll kr\log\log N$ in each of $O(\log\log N)$
dyadic blocks up to $N^A$.
\end{proof}
Whether Lemma~\ref{lem:firstnoB} removes $(\log\lambda_0)^{3/4}$ here is unchecked.
The next lemma (coordinator-checked only) concerns the literal
hypothesis of Corollary~\ref{cor:classorder}.

\begin{lemma}[packing; {\cite[Lemma~7.2]{KA3}}; \lab{proved}]\label{lem:packing}
Let $Z\geq2$ and $k\geq1$.  Every residue class $b\bmod d$ such that every
prime power $p^e\,\|\,d$ is $\leq Z$ and $\log d\leq(k-1)\log Z/2$ is an
intersection of at most $k$ residue classes of moduli $\leq Z$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Pack the prime powers $p^e\,\|\,d$, in any order, greedily (next-fit) into
bins whose products stay $\leq Z$; each fits into an empty bin.  Let there
be $m\geq2$ bins ($m\leq1$ is trivial), with products $b_1,\dots,b_m$.  The first
element of bin $i+1$ did not fit into bin $i$, so $b_ib_{i+1}>Z$ for $i<m$.
Hence $d\geq\prod_{j<\lfloor m/2\rfloor}b_{2j+1}b_{2j+2}>Z^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}$, so
$\lfloor m/2\rfloor<\log d/\log Z\leq(k-1)/2$ and $m\leq2\lfloor m/2\rfloor+1\leq k$.  The bins are
pairwise coprime with product $d$, and by CRT
$\{n\equiv b\ (d)\}=\bigcap_i\{n\equiv b\ (b_i)\}$.
\end{proof}
With $Z=N^A$, $L=\log N$: majorants with prime powers $\leq N^A$ and
$\log d_i\leq(k-1)AL/2$ satisfy the hypothesis of Corollary~\ref{cor:classorder},
which in turn forces level $\leq kAL$.  So, up to a factor $2$ in $k$, it is a
level hypothesis at $\lambda\asymp kL$.  Closing the window from it alone would
require $C[L^{3/4}+(\lambda/L)\,\mathrm{polylog}\,L]$ in place of $C\lambda^{3/4}$ for all such
majorants; the $k$-ary ledger cannot give this (it is optimal at
$s_1=\lambda^{1/4}\leq AL$, where truncation at $\e^{AL}$ is invisible), and whether
$\lambda^{3/4}$ is attained there is \lab{open} (\lab{evidence} only, \cite[\S2.5]{ET}).
The window is meaningful for structured orders: prime order (closed,
Corollary~\ref{cor:primeorder}) and intersections of $k$ \emph{family}
classes (closed for bounded $r=\max_G\omega_{>W}(G)$, Corollary~\ref{cor:boundedr}).
For the latter the open window is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:window}
 \max\bigl(L^{4\theta/3-1},\,L^\theta/(r\log L)\bigr)\lesssim k\lesssim L^\theta/\log L,
\end{equation}
nonempty only when $r\to\infty$ \cite[\S5]{KA3} \lab{open}.

\emph{Assessment} \cite[\S7]{KA3}.  In the pattern model of
Section~\ref{sec:kary}, class degree $k$ over $m$-prime patterns is degree $km$,
and binomial extrapolation (Lemma~\ref{lem:kextrap}) is tight as for prime
order $km$.  If this persists for forced classes, the window
\eqref{eq:window} is genuine; deciding it needs ES-specific input.

\subsection{Forced zeros and the repaired hypothesis}
\begin{lemma}[forms; {\cite[Lemma~1.1, Cor.~1.2]{TU2}}; \lab{proved}]\label{lem:forms}
Let $\ell\equiv3\pmod4$ be prime and $A=A_\ell$.  The map $(r,s,m)\mapsto D=r^2m$ is a
bijection from the triples with $rsm=A$, $(r,s)=1$, onto the divisors of
$A^2$, and $n\equiv-4D\pmod\ell$ if and only if $\ell\mid ns+r$.  In particular
$(1,1,A)$ gives the class $-1\bmod\ell$, which lies in $\cR(\ell)$ for every $\ell$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Write $D/A=r/s$ in lowest terms; then $A=sa'$, $D=ra'$, and $D\mid A^2$ gives
$r\mid a'$, so $a'=rm$.  The converse is clear.  As $4rsm=\ell+1$,
$4r^2m\equiv r/s\pmod\ell$.
\end{proof}
Call $(r,s)$ the \emph{form} of the class; fix one form per residue, $(1,1)$
for $-1$.  For a tuple $T$ let $T_\varphi$ be its pairs of form $\varphi=(r,s)$.  If
$\prod_{T_\varphi}\ell>Ns+r$ for some $\varphi$, then $C_T(N)=0$ although $\delta_T>0$, since
$\prod_{T_\varphi}\ell$ divides $ns+r\leq Ns+r$.  Call $T$ \emph{admissible} if this
happens for no $\varphi$; let $e_j^{\mathfrak A}$ be the CRT mass of admissible
$j$-tuples and $Z_j=e_j-e_j^{\mathfrak A}$ that of the \emph{forced zeros}.  Then
$S_j(N)-Ne_j=\sum_{T\ {\rm adm.},\,|T|=j}(C_T-N\delta_T)-NZ_j$.

\begin{theorem}[forced-zero mass; {\cite[Thms~2.1, 2.2]{TU2}}; \lab{proved}]\label{thm:forcedmass}
Fix $\theta\in(2/3,1)$, take $K=K_N$, $y=y_K$ as in Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:TC}, and
$u_0=\lceil\log(N+2)/\log(y/2)\rceil$.  For $N\geq N_0(\theta)$, $u_0\leq K$ and
$Z_{u_0}\geq\eta_K\exp\{K/(2\e^2)\}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Let $\mathcal Q$ be the primes $\ell\equiv3\pmod4$ in $(y/2,y]$, $\sigma_y=\sum_{\mathcal Q}1/\ell\geq c/\log y$.
A $u_0$-set $U\subseteq\mathcal Q$ with class $-1$ at each prime has $\prod_U\ell>N+1$, so is
a forced zero, and $Z_{u_0}\geq e_{u_0}((1/\ell)_{\mathcal Q})\geq(\sigma_y/2)^{u_0}/u_0!$.  Here
$u_0\asymp(\log N)^{1-\theta/2}$, so $\log Z_{u_0}\geq-C(\log N)^{1-\theta/2}\log\log N$, while
$\log(1/\eta_K)\asymp(\log N)^\theta$.  And $1-\theta/2<\theta$ for $\theta>2/3$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[a necessary condition; {\cite[Cor.~2.3]{TU2}}; \lab{proved}]\label{cor:TCexcess}
If $\theta\in(2/3,1)$, $N\geq N_0(\theta)$ and $\mathrm{TC}(N;K_N,y_K,\eta_K)$ holds, then
$\sum_{T\ {\rm adm.},\,|T|=u_0}(C_T(N)-N\delta_T)\geq N(Z_{u_0}-\eta_K)>0$: the admissible
$u_0$-tuples carry an aggregate excess over their CRT values.
\end{corollary}
\emph{Assessment} \cite[Ass.~3.2]{TU2}.  For the class $-1$ the count
$\lfloor(N+1)/q\rfloor$ is never above $(N+1)/q$ \cite[Lemma~3.1]{TU2}, so the
excess must come from multi-form tuples.  It is $N^{1/2-o(1)}$ times a
square-root noise scale, and no mechanism for it is known.  We expect
Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:TC} to be false for every $\theta\in(2/3,1)$; this is not
proved.  Toy moments on $[1,N]$ fall below CRT but not on far translates
\cite[\S5]{TU2} \lab{evidence}.

Theorem~\ref{thm:TCbrun} uses only the alternating sum, i.e.\ the Brun
pure-sieve majorant $\nu_K(n)=\sum_{j\leq K}(-1)^j\binom{f_y(n)}j$ ($K$ even).

\begin{hypothesis}[$\mathrm{TC}^{\rm alt}_\theta$; {\cite[\S4]{TU2}}; \lab{open}]\label{hyp:TCalt}
With $K=K_N$, $y=y_K$, $\eta=\eta_K$ as in Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:TC}, for all large $N$,
\[
 \sum_{n\leq N}\nu_K(n)\leq N\Bigl(2\prod_{\ell\in\cP_y}(1-p_\ell)+2\e^{-K}\Bigr)+K\eta N.
\]
\end{hypothesis}
The CRT mean of $\nu_K$ lies in $[\prod(1-p_\ell),\prod(1-p_\ell)+\e^{-K}]$, so this is a
one-sided statement with a factor $2$, not an accuracy statement.  The
intermediate hypothesis $\mathrm{TC}^{\mathfrak A}_\theta$ asks
$|S_j(N)-Ne_j^{\mathfrak A}|\leq\eta_KN$ for $j\leq K$: accuracy on admissible tuples only.

\begin{theorem}[Euler-characteristic cancellation; {\cite[Thm~4.1, Cor.~4.2]{TU2}}; \lab{proved}]\label{thm:euler}
Let $K\geq\e^2\mu_y$ be even, $u_1=\lfloor\log N/\log y\rfloor+1\geq4(1+\log y)$ and
$\varepsilon=4(2\e(1+\log y)/u_1)^{u_1}\leq1$.  Then
$|\sum_{j\leq K}(-1)^je_j^{\mathfrak A}-\prod(1-p_\ell)|\leq2\varepsilon\prod(1-p_\ell)+\e^{-K}$, and
$|\sum_{j\leq K}(-1)^jZ_j|\leq2\varepsilon\prod(1-p_\ell)+2\e^{-K}$.  Consequently, for
$\theta\in(0,1)$ and $N\geq N_0(\theta)$, $\mathrm{TC}^{\mathfrak A}_\theta$ implies
$E(N)\leq(\e+3)N\exp\{-(2/\e^2)(\log N)^\theta\}$, and Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:TCalt}
implies $E(N)\leq(2\e+2)N\exp\{-(2/\e^2)(\log N)^\theta\}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
For the CRT hit set $H$, $\sum_j(-1)^je_j^{\mathfrak A}=\E\sum_{T\subseteq H\ {\rm adm.},\,|T|\leq K}(-1)^{|T|}$,
and dropping $|T|\leq K$ costs $\leq\sum_{j>K}e_j\leq\e^{-K}$.  Admissibility is a
condition on each form group, so the full sum is $\prod_\varphi\chi_\varphi(H_\varphi)$ with
$\chi_\varphi(G)=\sum_{U\subseteq G\ {\rm adm.}}(-1)^{|U|}$.  Subsets of admissible sets are
admissible, so $\chi_\varphi(G)=\ind[G=\emptyset]$ for admissible $G$, and always
$|\chi_\varphi(G)|\leq2^{|G|}$.  So the sum differs from $\ind[H=\emptyset]$ only if every
nonempty group is inadmissible, hence has $\geq u_1$ primes.  With $p_\ell<1/2$
and $\sum_{\ell\equiv-1\,(4rs)}4/\ell\leq2(1+\log y)/(rs)$, the tails summed over forms give
$2\varepsilon$.  In the calibration $u_1/\log y_K\to\infty$, so $\varepsilon\to0$; conclude as in
Theorem~\ref{thm:TCbrun}.
\end{proof}
The second implication is just $\nu_K\geq\ind[f_y=0]$: Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:TCalt}
is essentially the conclusion restricted to one majorant (its factor $2$
matters: at $N=10^7$, $y=1000$ the avoiders on $[1,N]$ are $1.22$ times CRT,
an excess of about the $\sqrt N$ squares, \lab{evidence}).  It fails for even
$K\geq(\e^2/2+\varepsilon)\log N$ as in Proposition~\ref{prop:TCrange}(2).  That it is the
correct form of the tuple-count door is an \emph{Assessment}
\cite[\S4]{TU2}: forced zeros provably cancel in it, single-form floor
deficits heuristically do; $\mathrm{TC}^{\mathfrak A}_\theta$ is expected false above $2/3$.

\subsection{The prime family at \texorpdfstring{$2/3$}{2/3}}
\begin{corollary}[{\cite[Cor.~6.1]{TU2}}; \lab{proved}]\label{cor:prime23}
Fix $A\geq1$ and $y\leq N^A$.  Let $\nu$ be a CRT majorant of $\{f_y=0\}$ whose
terms have $\sum_{\ell\in\cP_y,\,\ell\mid d_i,\,\ell\geq\ell_0}\log\ell\leq\lambda$, $\lambda\geq\log N$.  Then
$\log(1/\E\nu)\leq C_A\lambda^{2/3}$.  So CRT-main-term methods at level
$\leq A'\log N$ save at most $C(\log N)^{2/3}$, the order of Brun's pure sieve
(Proposition~\ref{prop:TCrange}(1)).
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Put the primes $\ell<\ell_0$ into $Q_0$; then $|R|/Q_0\gg1$ and every slice prime
has $p_\ell\leq\tau(A_\ell^2)/\ell\leq1/4$.  Apply Theorem~\ref{thm:truncslice} with $k=1$,
$L_0=\lambda_0=\lambda$.  By $\mu_x\leq C(\log x)^2$ and partial summation,
$\sum_\ell p_\ell\ell^{-\alpha}\leq2C\alpha^{-2}$: the profile is quadratic, not cubic.  Take
$\alpha=\lambda^{-1/3}$.
\end{proof}
So the prime family's CRT exponent is exactly $2/3$, where literal
$\mathrm{TC}_\theta$ starts to see forced zeros.  Hence Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:TCalt} for
$\theta>2/3$ is non-CRT: a saving $(\log N)^\theta$ needs level $\geq c(\log N)^{3\theta/2}$, so
$\nu_K$ must be CRT-accurate on $[1,N]$ at moduli $\exp\{c(\log N)^{3\theta/2}\}$ (Brun's
sieve far beyond its level of distribution; it improves $E(N)$ only for
$\theta>3/4$, by \cite{TQ}).  That no known theorem reaches this level is an
\emph{Assessment} \cite[Ass.~6.2]{TU2}.

\subsection{What is open}
\begin{itemize}
\item Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:TCalt} for some $\theta>3/4$ (it would give $\theta>3/4$); no
  structured obstruction below $\theta=1$ is known.
\item The window \eqref{eq:window}: a $k$-ary comparison in class
  coordinates, for the dependent indicators $\ind[n\in C]$.
\item Interval deficits (the second branch of the dichotomy of
  Section~\ref{sec:noncrt}); fixed-shift statements combined over all
  shift tuples; non-witness information along shifts; bounded-order
  input with weights below $1$.
\end{itemize}

\section{What the theorems exclude}\label{sec:excluded}

The following list is checked against the proofs of
Theorems~\ref{thm:slice}, \ref{thm:seq}, \ref{thm:gapped},
\ref{thm:wseq}, \ref{thm:karycap}, \ref{thm:noBcap}, \ref{thm:main} and
Lemma~\ref{lem:budget} \cite[\S6.1]{ET}, \cite[\S4.3]{KA}, \cite[\S6]{NC},
\cite[\S6]{KA2}, and updated with Sections~\ref{sec:LS}
and~\ref{sec:TU} \cite[\S7]{LS}, \cite[\S0]{LS2}, \cite[\S0]{IF2},
\cite[\S6]{TU}, \cite[\S0]{TU2}, \cite[\S5]{KA3}.  Anything below lies outside
the cap of Theorem~\ref{thm:main}; where another theorem covers part of an
item, this is said.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{The large sieve at super-polynomial frequency levels.}
  Covered: every CRT-admissible large sieve with rational frequencies of
  polynomial level, fibrewise or not, with any weights and any moduli
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:LScap}); multiplicative, Gauss-sum, twisted and
  hybrid Bessel forms with periodic rows (Theorem~\ref{thm:hybrid}); the
  large sieve applied to the primes (Theorem~\ref{thm:LSprimes}); prime
  slices with any rational frequencies (Theorem~\ref{thm:LSslice}).
  Outside: frequencies of super-polynomial level whose denominators
  carry many family primes (escape (E1), Conjecture~\ref{conj:HLS}).  By
  Theorem~\ref{thm:band} this cannot be closed by comparison measures
  and large-sieve axioms alone: a proof must use the sparsity of forced
  classes.  Non-periodic rows (such as $n^{it}$) are non-CRT input.
\item \emph{The larger sieve with bad composite kernels.}  Gallagher's
  sieve with prime-power kernels saves at most $26\log\log N+C$ over any
  mixture, unconditionally (Theorem~\ref{thm:gallagher}); composite
  kernels are capped up to the factor $1+Nh/(W_K-h)$
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:compkernel}).  Outside: kernels for which that factor
  exceeds $\e^{(\log N)^{3/4}}$ and whose moduli have small prime factors.
\item \emph{Cancellation between frequencies above modulus $N/2$.}
  Covered: majorants built from classes of modulus $\leq N/2$, with any
  coefficients and any evaluation of the interval sum (dispersion,
  Kloosterman, Erd\H{o}s--Tur\'an/Vaaler, floor/ceiling, smooth windows)
  (Corollary~\ref{cor:IFcap}); bounds that dominate the large
  coefficient mass up to $\e^{O((\log N)^{3/4})}$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:IFbudget});
  hybrid methods whose free large mass is $\leq cB$
  (Section~\ref{sec:hybrid}).  Outside: hybrids with large right-signed
  mass at moduli in $(N/2,CN]$, and all hybrids unless Flat holds
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:hybridcap}; Flat follows from the combinatorial
  Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:SPW}, which is \lab{open}); direct counts of
  multi-witness patterns of combined modulus $>N/2$.  For finite prime-slice
  families every per-frequency rounding with weight $\geq1$ is covered
  for any moduli (Theorem~\ref{thm:perfreq}).
\item \emph{Per-frequency weights below one} with classes of modulus
  $>N/2$: covered only under Theorem~\ref{thm:IFbudget}'s hypothesis;
  for smooth windows on prime slices, \lab{conditional} on $(H_{\rm eq})$
  (Section~\ref{sec:noncrt}).
\item \emph{Majorants $\geq1$ only on part of the avoider set.}
  Theorem~\ref{thm:main} needs $\nu\geq0$ on $\Z$ and $\nu\geq1$ on all of
  $\A(\cG)\subseteq\Z$.  Covered: majorants $\geq0$ at all primes and $\geq1$ at
  the primes of $\A$, for every mixture, hence prime-law methods with
  BV/BDH/EH/GRH-type input at polynomial level (Theorem~\ref{thm:PLcap},
  Corollary~\ref{cor:PLmethods}).  The relaxation ``$\nu\geq1$ only at the
  primes of $\A\cap[1,N]$'' admits no cap of any kind
  (Proposition~\ref{prop:finiterange}): the gap there is certification,
  not majorant design.  Outside: $\nu\geq0$ only on $[1,N]$
  (Remark~\ref{rem:positivity}); $\nu\geq1$ only on $\A\cap[1,N]$ or on the
  exceptional set; $\nu\geq0$ only at primes $\leq N$ (with $\nu\geq1$ at all
  primes of $\A$), which is open (\emph{Assessment}); unconditional
  signed error terms for prime laws (\emph{Assessment}).
\item \emph{Non-CRT input.}  The theorems see only the CRT law of
  residues.  Covered: CRT-evaluated moment methods
  (Section~\ref{sec:noncrt}), correlation input of bounded order or of
  prime order $o((\log N)^\theta/\log\log N)$ under CRT-main-term evaluation
  (Corollaries~\ref{cor:classorder} and~\ref{cor:primeorder}), and the
  large sieve for primes (Theorem~\ref{thm:LSprimes}).  Outside:
  Type~I/II sums, the arithmetic of $(p+a)/4$, Hal\'asz-type arguments,
  non-witness divisor information along fixed shifts, tuple counts whose
  main term is not the CRT density (any $\theta>3/4$ input of this kind
  for the prime family needs accuracy at moduli $\exp\{c(\log N)^{3\theta/2}\}$,
  see the discussion after Corollary~\ref{cor:prime23}), and family-class order $k$ in the window
  \eqref{eq:window} for families whose moduli have unboundedly many
  primes above $W$ (Corollary~\ref{cor:boundedr},
  Lemma~\ref{lem:packing}).
\item \emph{Other class types}, including non-selector admissible sets.
  The proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap} uses only three properties of
  the classes: (a) no $W$-smooth class contains a unit square modulo its
  modulus (for the base); (b) first moments $\ll(\log y)^3$ over $P(G)\leq y$;
  (c) cofactor second moments $\ll\ell^{o(1)}$.  The same proof covers any
  family with (a)--(c) \cite[\S6, item~2]{KA2}; families without them are
  outside.  Any admissible set that cannot be expressed by selector
  classes (or by classes of the four types) has an uncontrolled term
  $\log(Q_0/|R|)$ (Remark~\ref{rem:Rterm}; \cite[\S6]{ET}); this includes
  admissible sets that exclude classes failing (a), such as classes of
  unit squares.  The one exception in this note is the quadratic-residue
  set $R_W$ for fixed-$B$ families of $\cR(M)$-classes
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:karycap}).  Schinzel's theorem, as quoted in
  \cite{ElsholtzTao}, may make (a) automatic for polynomial-identity
  families; this has not been checked.
\item \emph{Primes beyond $N^{O(1)}$.}  Families with primes beyond
  $N^{O(1)}$ are outside Theorem~\ref{thm:main}, since
  Lemma~\ref{lem:budget} needs $\Lambda_0$.  Covered without such a bound:
  finite prime-slice families with per-frequency rounding
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:perfreq}), majorants from classes of modulus $\leq N/2$
  (Corollary~\ref{cor:IFcap}), and large sieves on prime slices with any
  rational frequencies (Theorem~\ref{thm:LSslice}).
\end{enumerate}
The exact exponent is now settled for the class of
Theorem~\ref{thm:main}: the factor $(\log\log N)^{3/4}$ of the previous
version is gone (Lemma~\ref{lem:firstnoB}), so no gain
$(\log N)^{3/4}\omega(N)$ with $\omega\to\infty$ is possible in that class, with or
without $B$.  The same holds for the results of Section~\ref{sec:LS} that
use Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap} as a black box; for the prime-measure results
(Theorem~\ref{thm:PLcap} and what rests on it) the improvement is
pointer-level only \cite[\S4.3]{KA3}.  All constants are absolute but
astronomically large (Section~\ref{sec:noB}).

\emph{No longer excluded} (compared with version~2 of this note).  The
following are inside Theorem~\ref{thm:main}: $\cR(M)$-moduli with
$\log M/\log P(M)$ unbounded; $(a,D)$- and Case-A moduli without a dominant
prime; families mixing all four types; twin moduli
(Lemma~\ref{lem:balancedsupply}); any number of prime factors per modulus;
selector admissible sets (as selector classes, with selector primes
$\leq N^A$); and multiplier conditions with a small part larger than the
top prime, used without a separate level budget, provided the conditions
are themselves classes of the four types and all their primes, those of
the multiplier included, are $\leq N^A$.  For these,
Theorem~\ref{thm:main} charges every prime above $W$ and bounds the level
through $\sum_i|a_i|<N$ together with the bound $N^A$ on the family primes.
\emph{New in this version}: the factor $(\log\log N)^{3/4}$ without $B$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}); the large sieve beyond prime slices at
polynomial level, its twisted, hybrid and prime forms, and Gallagher's
larger sieve (Section~\ref{sec:LS}); prime-only majorants for all mixtures;
interval counts with all moduli $\leq N/2$; bounded-order and
prime-order tuple input; and the $\Lambda^2$ middle range $r\asymp\log L$ as a
cap (Remark~\ref{rem:L2vsmain}).  For the external input, the Case-A part
uses Elsholtz--Tao Prop.~1.4 and Thm~7.1, Cor.~7.4 and (7.10) of their
\S7 (published, not re-proved; Remark~\ref{rem:ETslips}), the latter as
Lemma~\ref{lem:smoothA} does; families without Case-A classes use only
Shiu's theorem.

\begin{lemma}[balanced moduli carry cubic supply; {\cite[Lemma~3.8]{ET}}; \lab{proved}]\label{lem:balancedsupply}
There are $c>0$ and $x_0$ such that for $x\geq x_0$,
\[
 \sum_{M\leq x,\ M\equiv3\ (4),\ P(M)\leq M^{1/2}}\frac{|\cR(M)|}M\geq c(\log x)^3.
\]
More precisely, for each fixed $\eta\in(0,1/480]$, the moduli $M=k\ell_1\ell_2$
with primes $\ell_1\in(Y,Y^{1+\eta/2}]$, $\ell_2\in(Y^{1+\eta/2},Y^{1+\eta}]$, odd
$k\in(Y^{2\eta},Y^{3\eta}]$ and $Y=x^{1/(2+4.5\eta)}$ already contribute
$\geq c(\eta)(\log x)^3$; these are same-scale pairs,
$\log\ell_2/\log\ell_1\leq1+\eta$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Each such $M$ is $\leq x$ and balanced, and determines $(k,\ell_1,\ell_2)$.
For coprime $u,v\leq z=Y^{1/8}$ with $(uv,k)=1$ and $4uv\mid M+1$, the class
$-uv^{-1}\pmod M$ lies in $\cR(M)$, and distinct $(u,v)$ give distinct
classes modulo $\ell_2$.  The $\ell_2$-sum over the progression
$-(k\ell_1)^{-1}\pmod{4uv}$ is evaluated by partial summation, with the
error terms controlled on average by Cauchy--Schwarz against
Brun--Titchmarsh and the Bombieri--Vinogradov theorem
\cite[Ch.~28]{Davenport}.  The $(u,v)$-sum is bounded below by the
lattice lemma of \cite[Lemma~3.1]{LL}, which needs $\eta\leq1/480$.  The
$k$- and $\ell_1$-sums give $\gg\eta\log Y$ and $\gg\eta$.  Full details are
in \cite[\S3.8]{ET}, checked in \cite[item~4]{ETrev}.
\end{proof}
The share of the weighted Case-B mass carried by balanced moduli is
$0.098$, $0.121$, $0.141$, $0.157$ and $0.171$ at $x=10^3,\dots,10^7$,
still growing \lab{evidence}.  Lemma~\ref{lem:balancedsupply} does not by itself
show that every window partition loses: singleton windows have no
internal pairs.  So the twin moduli had to be covered, not discarded.
For fixed $B$ they cannot beat the cap (Theorem~\ref{thm:karycap})
\lab{proved}; without the $B$-hypothesis they are inside
Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap}, with the same cap $C\lambda^{3/4}$ \lab{proved}.

\section{Open problems}\label{sec:open}

A proof of $\theta>3/4$ by a congruence sieve must therefore contain at
least one of the following \cite[\S6]{ET}, \cite[\S4.3]{KA},
\cite[\S6]{NC}, \cite[\S6]{KA2}, \cite[\S7]{LS}, \cite[\S0]{LS2},
\cite[\S0]{IF2}, \cite[\S0]{TU2}, \cite[\S5]{KA3}:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] classes of a type outside Definition~\ref{def:types} that fail
  one of the properties (a)--(c) of Section~\ref{sec:excluded}, item~7,
  or a non-selector admissible set with a large saving of its own;
\item[(ii)] a majorant that is $\geq1$ only on part of the avoider set
  (on $[1,N]$ or on the exceptional set), or a prime majorant that is
  $\geq0$ only at primes $\leq N$;
\item[(iii)] large-sieve frequencies of super-polynomial level used
  against the sparsity of forced classes (Conjecture~\ref{conj:HLS} would
  exclude this), or larger-sieve kernels with an uncontrolled factor
  $1+Nh/(W_K-h)$;
\item[(iv)] a direct count above modulus $N/2$: a hybrid method that
  counts classes of modulus in $(N/2,CN]$ exactly with large right-signed
  mass, or any hybrid at those $N$ for which Flat fails
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:hybridcap}; Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:SPW} would exclude this), or
  cancellation between frequencies of combined modulus $>N/2$;
\item[(v)] a non-CRT tuple count: Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:TCalt} for some
  $\theta>3/4$, i.e.\ CRT-accuracy on $[1,N]$ at moduli
  $\exp\{c(\log N)^{3\theta/2}\}$; or family-class order in the window
  \eqref{eq:window} for moduli with many primes above $W$; or other
  non-CRT input (Type~I/II sums, Hal\'asz-type arguments);
\item[(vi)] family primes beyond $N^{O(1)}$, outside the settings of
  Theorem~\ref{thm:perfreq}, Corollary~\ref{cor:IFcap} and
  Theorem~\ref{thm:LSslice}.
\end{enumerate}
Gone since version~2: moduli with $\log M/\log P(M)$ unbounded, $(a,D)$- and
Case-A moduli without a dominant prime, mixed families, and multipliers
larger than the slice primes (provided the multiplier conditions are
classes of the four types with all primes $\leq N^A$); unresolved
$\eta$-twin moduli, item (i) of the list in \cite[\S6]{ET}.  Gone since
version~3: the large sieve beyond prime slices at polynomial level, in
twisted, hybrid and prime forms; Gallagher's larger sieve; prime-only
majorants of mixtures; per-frequency rounding and inter-frequency
cancellation when all moduli are $\leq N/2$; tuple input of bounded or
prime order; and the factor $(\log\log N)^{3/4}$.

\emph{The exact exponent without $B$} is settled for the class of
Theorem~\ref{thm:main}: the factor $(\log\lambda)^{3/4}$ of the previous version
is removed by the local-weight moment (Lemma~\ref{lem:firstnoB}).  The
heuristic bound $\sum_{M\leq x,\,P(M)\leq y}\tau(A_M^2)\ll\Psi(x,y)(\log x)^2$ for
shifted smooth numbers \cite[Remark~3.8]{KA2} was not needed and remains
\lab{open}; it would replace the crude decay $u^{-4}$ of
Lemma~\ref{lem:smoothR} by a $\rho(u)$-type decay, which does not affect the
cap.

\emph{Constants.}  All constants of Section~\ref{sec:noB} are absolute but
astronomically large; the saving of \cite{TQ} has an ineffective constant.
Comparing them is \lab{open} and not attempted.

The following statement would close (i) for forced classes with a
bounded-saving admissible set.

\begin{hypothesis}[$H_{\rm MS}$; {\cite[\S5.6]{ET}}; \lab{open}]\label{hyp:MS}
For every CRT system of forced classes with moduli $\leq\e^\lambda$, every
majorant of level $\lambda$ satisfies
$\log(1/\E\nu)\leq C\inf_\alpha\bigl[\alpha\lambda+\sum_{\rm cond}\Prob({\rm cond})({\rm mod\ cond})^{-\alpha}\bigr]+O(\log^2\lambda)$.
\end{hypothesis}
Under $H_{\rm MS}$ every CRT-majorant architecture built from Case-B and
Case-A forced classes, with a bounded-saving admissible set and rounding
treated in absolute value, is capped at $(\log N)^{3/4}$
\lab{conditional}.  $H_{\rm MS}$ is open even for one scale of primes with
random pair conditions.

For the twin residual, the reduction of \cite[\S6]{TW} isolated an
arithmetic-free statement.  Let $V$ be a block of primes with costs
$s_\ell\in(s,(1+\eta)s]$ and $d=\lfloor\lambda/s\rfloor$.

\begin{conjecture}[$k$-ary comparison inequality; {\cite[Conj.~6.4]{TW}}; \lab{open}]\label{conj:kary}
Let $\nu=\bigotimes\nu_\ell$ be a product law on $V$, and let $\mathcal F$ be a
family of hard $k$-ary constraints ($2\leq k\leq r$) on $V$ with all incident
weights $\leq\theta\leq\theta_0(r)$ and total $\nu$-mass $\mu_{\geq2}$.  Let
$\sigma$ be the sequential law: in increasing order of $\ell$, $\nu_\ell$
conditioned off the residues activated by earlier coordinates, or $\nu_\ell$
itself if that set has $\nu_\ell$-mass $>\delta_\ell$.  Then for every
$\lambda$-level $f\geq0$,
\[
 \E_\sigma f\leq\exp\bigl(C_r[d\log(2+\mu_{\geq2})+1]\bigr)\,\E_\nu f.
\]
\end{conjecture}
Here the \emph{incident weight} of a residue $a$ at $\ell$ is the
$\nu$-probability that some activated constraint through $(\ell,a)$ is
completed by the other coordinates.

The statement of Conjecture~\ref{conj:kary} is the \emph{unweighted}
form.  Its weighted form, for the same law $\sigma$, is
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcomp} \lab{proved}, and without the incident-weight
hypothesis the unweighted form is false (Remark~\ref{rem:kweighted}).
With it, the unweighted form remains \lab{open}, but it is no longer
needed for the $3/4$ question \cite[\S5]{KA}.

\begin{proposition}[{\cite[Prop.~6.5]{TW}}; \lab{conditional} on Conjecture~\ref{conj:kary} and Hypothesis~K2]\label{prop:twinconditional}
Assume Conjecture~\ref{conj:kary} for $r=\lfloor(1+B)(1+\eta)\rfloor$ and the
$k$-ary second-moment hypothesis K2 of \cite[\S6.3]{TW}.  Then for every
fixed $B$, every family of $\cR(M)$-classes with $M\leq P(M)^{1+B}$ (twin,
prime-power top and all other shapes included), plus $W_0$-smooth classes,
has saving $\ll_B\eta^{-1}\lambda^{3/4}\log\lambda$.
\end{proposition}
Its conclusion, without the factor $\log\lambda$ and without either
hypothesis, is Theorem~\ref{thm:karycap}.  Proposition~\ref{prop:twinconditional}
is kept only to record the route.

For $\Lambda^2$-majorants the core is a sparse noise-stability statement.

\begin{conjecture}[sparse noise stability; {\cite[Conj.~6.8]{TW}}; \lab{open}]\label{conj:sparseNS}
Let $X_i$ be independent and $\mathcal E$ a finite family of events, each
depending on coordinates $S(E)$, with incident weights
$\sum_{E\ni i}\Prob(E)\e^{a|S(E)|}\leq\theta\leq\theta_0(a)$ and a codegree
condition for $|S(E)|\geq3$.  Let $\A$ be ``no event'' and $\A'$ its
$\rho$-correlated copy.  Then
\[
 \log\frac{\Prob(\A\cap\A')}{\Prob(\A)^2}\leq(1+O(\theta))\sum_E\Prob(E)\Bigl(\prod_{i\in S(E)}(\rho_i+(1-\rho_i)P_i(E))-\Prob(E)\Bigr),
\]
where $P_i(E)$ is the marginal factor of $E$ at $i$.
\end{conjecture}
Theorem~\ref{thm:binaryNS} proves a bound of this type for binary systems
($|S(E)|\leq2$) with the diagonal and one-site terms on the right; the
any-arity bound of \cite[Lemmas~6.1, 6.2]{TW3}, with codegree terms and a
free codegree quarantine, is what Theorem~\ref{thm:rprime} uses.  The
conjecture itself is \lab{open}.

\emph{Many large primes in the $\Lambda^2$ route} \lab{open}.  The
$\Lambda^2$ caps of Section~\ref{sec:selberg} are uniform for
$r\leq\epsilon\log L/\log\log L$ prime factors above $(\log X)^8$, and the
moduli with $r\geq330\log L$ are free.  The middle range, which by the
\emph{Assessment} of \cite[\S9.3]{TW4} carries most
of the $\alpha^{-3}$-weighted mass, needs per-prime-efficient bounds for
the number of vertex hubs and for an off-diagonal pair sum
\cite[\S12]{TW4}.  This now concerns only the $\Lambda^2$ method:
Theorem~\ref{thm:karycap} under the $B$-hypothesis, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:noBcap} without it for majorants $\geq1$ on the whole
avoider set, cover every $r$ (Remark~\ref{rem:L2vsmain}).

\emph{Non-CRT counts} \lab{open}.  Conjecture $(H_{\rm eq})$ for smooth
windows and the local-limit hypothesis $H_{\rm node}$ for exact interval
counts (Section~\ref{sec:noncrt}) are open, as is any treatment of
non-symmetric hit-pattern majorants on $[1,N]$ \cite[\S8.6]{NC}.
